All five now require operator input (`extra.financialRows`, `extra.strengths`, `extra.strategies`, `ipo.promoters` or `extra.promoters`, `extra.objects`); when absent the corresponding section is omitted from the detail page. FAQs stay (they're generic help text answered with REAL IPO fields — name / minAmount / listingDate — not fabricated per-company claims).

## Conventions
- **Design = "calm & data-clear"** (Apple discipline + functional financial color). Neutral base, hairline borders, indigo on CTAs; **semantic green/red only for financial signals**. Listed chip = light purple; Print Forms CTA = light red `#fdebea`/`#b3372e`. **Never copy competitor UI** (IPOJI/IPOGuru studied for features, not looks).
- Reusable admin UI lives in `apps/web/components/ui` — incl. **SearchSelect** (type-to-filter combobox; list renders via portal because admin `.card` is `overflow:hidden`; also used on the consumer profile form for the UPI handle), **Toast** (top-right toasts), **Tooltip** (below), `.filter-row` (aligns filter fields + buttons; `.field` carries an 18px bottom margin).
- **`Tooltip`** (`components/ui/Tooltip.tsx`, 2026-09-23) — hover / focus / tap popover, **portalled to `body`** because `.ipocard` is `overflow:hidden` (its rounded corners clip the radial wash and the left rail), same reason SearchSelect portals out of the admin `.card`. Three things it got wrong first and now encodes:
  - **`render` is a FUNCTION, not a node.** A list page mounts sixteen of these; the contents (a subscription table each) must only be built while one is open.
  - **A scroll REPOSITIONS it; it does not close it.** Closing looks like the cheap option and is wrong — `HTMLElement.focus()` scrolls its target into view, so tabbing to an off-screen trigger opened the tooltip and the scroll that very focus caused dismissed it in the same tick. It closes only when the trigger leaves the viewport. Scroll/resize are coalesced to one measure per `requestAnimationFrame`.
  - **`z-index: 190`** — above the sticky header (50) and every dropdown (60–80), UNDER the modal overlay (200). A tooltip must never float over a dialog it cannot belong to.
  - Testing note: `el.focus()` is a **no-op when the window itself isn't focused**, which the Browser pane often isn't — a tooltip that "doesn't open" there may open fine for a user. Drive it with `.click()` instead.
- **Web and mobile keep the profile form in step by hand** (different frameworks, no shared component): split UPI input (name grows / handle fixed + filterable) and the one-line NSDL-CDSL explainer exist twice — `apps/web/components/Account.tsx` and `apps/mobile/app/profiles/new.tsx`. Change one, change the other. Widths differ on purpose (handle 158px web / 124px mobile — the phone row has less room).
- **One type contract** — shared types, the bid engine, the glossary, `format.ts` (`titleCase()`, `shortName()`, the `LABEL` vocabulary, `demandWord`), `stage.ts` AND the issue engine (`computeIssue.ts` + `issueRules.ts`) live in `packages/shared-types`. Web and mobile IMPORT these; never re-implement locally. Derived figures used by more than one surface belong in ONE helper too — `categoryLanes()` in `HeroBanner.tsx` feeds both IPO-slide treatments, `issueInputsFor()` feeds every issue derivation.
- **Nest sends an EMPTY body for a `null` handler result** — not the string `"null"` — so `res.json()` throws "Unexpected end of JSON input". Every web API client (`consumer-api.ts`, `tenants-admin.ts`) reads the body as text and treats an empty 200 as `null`. This broke `/partner/apply` for every first-time visitor.
- **`runUnscoped` is not enough on its own for cross-tenant reads on a pooled connection.** `PrismaService`'s tenant extension wraps scoped queries in `$transaction([set_config('app.current_tenant_id', <tid>, true), query])` — `is_local=true` means the GUC resets at transaction end, which works in isolation. But under load the pooled connection can be borrowed while the GUC still carries a value from a prior request, and `runUnscoped(() => findFirst(...))` just calls the query directly without touching the GUC — so RLS on `Application` / `InvestorProfile` silently filters the row out and the handler returns `null`. This 404'd the Print report's View modal for real rows that existed in the DB. Fix pattern: wrap the cross-tenant read in an explicit `$transaction([$executeRaw`SELECT set_config('app.current_tenant_id', ${scope ?? ''}, true)`, findFirst])` — deterministically resets (or narrows to `scope`) before the read, whatever state the connection was in. See `partner.service.ts printDetail` for the reference.
- **Cloudflare/WAF intercepts UUID-in-path patterns under `/prints/`.** `GET /admin/partner-api/prints/<uuid>` returned 404 "Cannot GET" at the browser (Nest default for unmatched routes) even though `curl http://127.0.0.1` from origin returned 401 — the intercept was returning the 404 upstream of iisnode. Moving the ID to a query parameter on a distinct literal path (`GET /print-detail?id=<uuid>`) sidesteps the intercept entirely. Sibling routes `/calls` and `/prints` (list) were unaffected — only the `/prints/<uuid>` shape triggered it.
- **CSS: check for a name collision before adding a class.** `globals.css` is one big global sheet and it has bitten FOUR times: `.sh` inherited a section utility's 76px padding and blew the banner 302→495px; `.nav-link` was defined for BOTH the front-site header and the admin sidebar, and the admin copy (later in the file) dragged `display:flex; gap:11px; width:100%` onto the site nav — the hole between "IPOs" and its count badge (admin rules now scoped to `.admin-side`); a candidate slide named `.steps` hit the apply-flow stepper's `margin: 10px 0 28px`, and since a stretched flex item's height is the container MINUS its margins, that slide rendered 38px short; `.gl-page` lost to `.trust-page`'s later `max-width`. Grep first, prefix banner/page classes (`hb-`, `hbn-`, `hbs-`, `hbp-`, `brd-`, `sd-`). The build catches none of it; **measure the live DOM**.
- **PROSE IN AN `auto` GRID TRACK SIZES THAT TRACK TO ITS OWN MAX-CONTENT — one sentence crushed a card to 69px** (2026-10-21, reported by the operator within the hour of shipping it). `.subv2-det-in`'s second and third tracks are `minmax(0, auto)` — **the third carries a 255px FLOOR, see the review section; it was 290 for a day and that starved Share-wise into a scrollbar** — and an **`auto` track sizes to MAX-CONTENT**: the width the content would take on ONE line. A 150-character footnote measures ~704px unwrapped, so it grew the Application-wise column from 274px to **734px** and left Share-wise, the `1fr` beside it, with **69px**.
  - **The fix is `width: 0; min-width: 100%`**, the "do not contribute to intrinsic sizing" idiom: during intrinsic sizing the percentage cannot resolve so the element contributes its `0`; during layout the track is definite so it fills the column and wraps. The column is sized by its TABLE again, which is what sized it before the note existed.
  - **WHY IT WAS MISSED, and this is the rule worth keeping: every width measured — 800, 1009, 375 — was at or below the 1100px breakpoint**, where that grid falls back to two `fr` tracks and an `fr` does NOT grow to max-content. The fault existed only ABOVE it, which is every desktop anybody actually uses. **The Browser pane's own width is not a desktop check — emulate 1440 before calling a grid change verified.** (The detail page was never affected: `.subv2-detail` overrides the grid to two `fr` tracks.)
- **A shared table rule reaches rows you never pictured.** `.fin-tab th:first-child { text-align: left }` exists for the Date column; the day-wise table's SECOND header row *starts* at `bHNI`, so bHNI was that row's `:first-child` and sat a whole column-width left of its own numbers, while `sHNI` (the second cell) looked fine — one header of a pair wrong is the tell. Two lessons: think about every `<tr>` a `:first-child` rule can land on, and **count specificity before assuming an override lands** — `.day-wise-sub thead tr + tr th` (0,1,3) LOSES to `.fin-tab th:first-child` (0,2,1); it took `.fin-tab.day-wise-sub thead tr + tr th` to win. Verify by resolving the computed value in the live DOM, not by reading the rule you just wrote.
- **`--neg-ink` (#b3372e) IS THE SAME PROMOTION ON THE RED SIDE, and it exists because `.banner.bad` WAS USED BY SIX CALL SITES AND DEFINED BY NONE** (2026-10-22). Every error banner in admin rendered as the plain neutral `.banner` — present, but not reading as an error, which is the quiet kind of CSS bug nobody reports. Measured in the SHIPPED bundle rather than read from source: **`--neg` on `--neg-soft` is 3.86:1 and FAILS AA** for body text, where `--neg-ink` is **5.19:1**, in line with `.banner.warn` at 5.89 and `.banner.ok` at 5.80. Like `--pos-ink` it promotes a hex already in the palette (the Print Forms CTA), rather than adding a tenth colour.
  - ~~**`.st.bad` STILL CARRIES THE FAILING PAIR**~~ **FIXED the same day** — it is `--neg-ink` now, **5.19:1 against a 4.5 bar at 11.5px**.
  - **GREPPING FOR THE PATTERN FOUND SEVEN MORE RULES, NOT ONE, AND THEY DO NOT ALL FAIL.** The AA bar depends on size (3:1 for large text, 4.5:1 otherwise) and on whether the thing is text at all, so each was measured in the live DOM rather than counted:
    - **PASSES, leave alone**: `.icon-btn.danger:hover` and `.row-actions .icon-btn.danger:hover` are ICONS, judged as UI components against **3:1**, which 3.86 clears.
    - **`.appstatus.bad` FIXED on the operator’s call** — 5.19:1 now. It is CUSTOMER-FACING and carries `not_allotted`, `rejected`, `failed` and `dp_failed` plus the Same PAN warning, so it is what nearly every unsuccessful applicant reads after an allotment. **`.gmp-pill.gn` DELIBERATELY LEFT at 3.86:1** (operator, 2026-10-22): the identical defect, but a decorative figure rather than the reason an application did not go through. **`.pill-time.urgent` is DEAD CSS** — nothing in the app references it — so it was never worth a deploy.
    - **I LABELLED TWO OF THESE “admin” WITHOUT CHECKING WHERE THEY RENDER, and the operator decided from that label.** `.appstatus` is used by Account, ApplyWizard and Portfolio — all consumer — and `.pill-time` is used nowhere at all. Caught only because the next step was to edit them. **A table of findings is a set of claims, and each cell needs the same grep the headline got.**
    - **`--tone-ink: var(--neg)` (the `.t-*` negative tone) IS DELIBERATELY NOT TOUCHED.** It is the status colour code — Closing Today, Closed and Withdrawn all read through it — and this file has a standing rule that a colour is never written next to a status by hand. Changing it is a change to the legend, not a contrast tweak.
  - **A CLASS THAT IS USED AND NOT DEFINED FAILS SILENTLY** — it inherits the base rule and looks deliberate. Worth a sweep: check each two-word modifier against the stylesheet rather than assuming the pair exists.
- **`--pos-ink` (#0c6b41) is for a LABEL on `--pos-soft`; `--pos` is for fills.** Added 2026-09-28 when the IPO-catalog *Complete* badge went from gold to pastel green (gold collided with Allotment Out in the status colour code, and a hard outline made a 10px chip read as a button). The obvious `color: var(--pos)` on `var(--pos-soft)` measures **3.50:1 — it FAILS WCAG AA for text**, and the gold it replaced was 5.75:1, so the pretty version was the less legible one. `--pos-ink` is 5.80:1 and was already the hex `.banner.ok` paired with that fill, so this is a token promotion, not a ninth palette. **Measure the pair before shipping a tint — a nicer chip that is harder to read is not an improvement.** Verified by resolving the `var()` chain in the SHIPPED bundle, per the rule below.
- **Prefer explicit values over "looks about right"** when two controls must match: the status track and the board meter are both pinned to a stated 40px (32px button + 3px padding + 1px border), not to padding that drifts the moment a font size changes.
- **Anchor jumps under a sticky bar need JS, not CSS.** `html` carries `scroll-padding-top` AND the target carries `scroll-margin-top`, and the two ADD — which parked glossary/FAQ headings 137px below the bar. `scrollToSection()` in `lib/useScrollSpy.ts` positions from the bar's **stuck** height (its sticky `top` + its own height); its live rect is wrong before it sticks, which is why the first click on a fresh page missed by 167px.
- **Two scroll gotchas that only appear at runtime**: `IntersectionObserver` scroll-spy picks the topmost *intersecting* section, which for tall sections is the one already scrolled PAST — read positions directly instead. And `scrollIntoView({ block: 'nearest' })` scrolls the nearest scrollable ancestor in the VERTICAL axis (the page), so using it to keep a chip visible in a horizontal strip fights any smooth scroll in flight — set `scrollLeft` instead.
- **`requestAnimationFrame` does not fire in a hidden tab.** Throttle scroll handlers on a timestamp; the Browser pane reports `document.hidden` and `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce`, so rAF loops and CSS animations cannot be observed there — verify their wiring, not their motion.
- **Web ≠ mobile by design**: web is the research + SEO surface (density, tables, full detail, trust signals, conversion for anonymous visitors); mobile is the task surface (speed, one CTA per stage). What must stay identical: vocabulary, status wording, Title-Case names, semantic colours, the GMP disclaimer.
- **Status colour is a COLOUR CODE: one colour per status, no two statuses share one.** `STAGE_TONE` in `packages/shared-types/src/stage.ts` is the single map — Live/Open Today **green `#12925a`** · **Closing Today · Closed · Withdrawn red `#d8412a`** · Pre Apply **light indigo `#7565bd`** · Upcoming **indigo `#3c2e7e`** · Awaiting Allotment **teal `#2b8a96`** (was grey; changed 2026-09-02 — grey read as an absence of status rather than a status, and teal is the one family no other status uses) · Allotment Out **gold `#e6ad12`** · Listed **purple `#9b7fd4`**. TWO families share a colour and only two: Open Today *is* Live on day one, and Closing Today / Closed / Withdrawn are one event caught at different moments. Inside a family the card separates states with a `solid` fill, never a second colour. Web reads it through CSS variables (`.t-*` sets `--tone` · `--tone-soft` · `--tone-ink` · `--tone-solid`/`--tone-on` · `--tone-acc`); raw status/phase class names (`.ph.live`, `.ic-dot.open`, `.status.upcoming`…) are **aliases on those same rules**. **Never write a colour next to a status name.** Closing Today is the one status allowed to borrow the loss red — that is a deliberate exception to "semantic green/red only for financial signals".
- **Do not group statuses by what they MEAN.** An earlier attempt used five semantic tones (Closing Today and Allotment Out were both "act today", so both gold) — which left two different statuses as the same swatch side by side in the Today row, and took three rounds of the operator repeating himself before it was understood. The operator wants a legend, not a mood. `stage.spec.ts` now asserts that no colour serves two statuses (Open Today excepted) and that the four Today-row swatches are all distinct.
- **An EIGHTH palette surfaced on 2026-09-02**: the IPO card's left rail (`.ipocard.st-*::after`) and the detail hero's (`.detail-hero.st-*::after`) each hand-wrote three colours off the RAW status — `.st-upcoming` gold, `.st-listed` light indigo — so an Upcoming card wore Allotment Out's gold beside an indigo chip, and four statuses had no rule at all and fell through to grey. Both now read `var(--tone)` from the card root's `.t-*` class, so a rail cannot disagree with the chip beside it. **When you add a status-coloured surface, give its root the tone class; never key off `st-`.**
- **This had drifted across SEVEN palettes** before it was first unified (2026-08-28): four byte-identical dot palettes on web alone (`.mkt-n` · `.sd-n` · `.flt-status` · `.h2b-dot`), plus `.ts-dot`, the card chips, *both* banner tag sets, `.ph` (admin), `.ic-dot`, `.status`, and three separate maps on mobile. `.ph.upcoming` was yellow in admin; the pastel banner slide painted Closing Today red while the dark slide painted it gold. **Verify by resolving the `var()` chains in the SHIPPED bundle** — reading source missed that `--tone` and `--tone-solid` were two different golds.
- **Vocabulary** (customer-facing): Offer Price · Lot Size · Min Application · Issue Size · Lot Details · Reservation · Key Dates · Subscribed · Allotment Status · Listing Performance · Opening Soon. Statuses: Opens in Nd · Pre Apply · Open Today · Live · Closing Today · **Awaiting Allotment** · **Allotment Out** · Listed. SEO meta copy deliberately keeps "price band" — that's the search term.
- **IPO names** are ALL CAPS from registrars → always render through `titleCase()` (keeps HDFC/SBI/NTPC, fixes honorifics, handles 5Paisa), or `shortName()` on dense surfaces.
- **"Exp. Premium" is the customer-facing term on BOTH surfaces** (operator's call, 2026-09-02) and it lives in ONE place — `LABEL.gmp` in `format.ts`, read by web cards/hubs/nav/detail and by all five mobile sites, so the two can no longer drift. Three things deliberately still say GMP: the `/gmp` **URL and SEO copy** (that is the search term, same reasoning as keeping "price band"), **`LABEL.gmpLong` = "Grey Market Premium"** for the disclaimer, which has to name the thing for what it is, and the **mobile compliance dialog** ("Before you view GMP figures…").
- **Third-party marks**: the Android robot and the Play/App Store badges carry Google's brand conditions, and a store badge implies a listing that doesn't exist yet. The `/home2` app teaser therefore uses **our own** app-icon squircle (the banner's candle motif) and only the *word* Android — no attribution line needed. Swap in the real badge on the day there is a listing behind it.
- Web IPO list has **two layouts**: the card grid (**default**, operator decision) and `IpoCompareTable` (sortable, toggle on desktop); the choice persists in `localStorage`.
- **The site can be verified from this box** with the Browser pane (`preview_start` → `javascript_exec` to measure the live DOM). Cloudflare 403s server-side `curl` to the public host — hit `http://127.0.0.1` with `-H 'Host: newipoapi.finwave.co'` to test the API locally.
  - **`curl --resolve <host>:443:127.0.0.1` IS NOT A VALID LIVENESS TEST ON THIS BOX, AND IT REPORTED A FOUR-HOST OUTAGE THAT DID NOT EXIST** (2026-10-15). A binding made in IIS Manager with a specific IP selected is `192.168.46.88:443:<host>`, and **loopback does not match an IP-specific binding** — so http.sys answers its OWN 404 while the site serves the internet perfectly. The identical command had worked that morning only because the binding it happened to be testing was `*:443:`. **Probe 192.168.46.88 as well as 127.0.0.1 and treat the host as served if EITHER answers**; `scripts/bind-investoyard-domain.ps1` enumerates the local addresses and does exactly that.
    - **THE DISCRIMINATOR BETWEEN "NOT BOUND" AND "BAD CONTENT" IS WHICH LOG THE 404 LANDS IN, and it settles the question in one command.** http.sys answers before IIS ever sees the request, so an unregistered host appears in `C:\Windows\System32\LogFiles\HTTPERR` as **`404 - NotFound`** and in **NO** site log under `C:\inetpub\logs\LogFiles`. A content 404 is the exact opposite — in the site log, absent from HTTPERR. **Request a unique probe path and grep every site log for it**: if no site logged it, stop looking at content and look at bindings.
    - **A SITE LOG ALSO TELLS YOU WHICH SITE OWNS A HOST** — the `cs-host` field, or failing that `cs(Referer)`. W3SVC6's last line naming `https://investoyard.com/...` is what proved the site was alive and receiving that host at the moment loopback said it was dead.
    - **`netsh http show sslcert` WORKS UNELEVATED** and lists the SNI certificate per hostname, which separates a TLS fault from a routing one. It is also what identified the new domain's certificate as a Sectigo wildcard living in `LocalMachine\WebHosting` and NOT in `My` — so **search every store before concluding a binding points at a certificate that does not exist**. That certificate carries BOTH `*.investoyard.com` and `investoyard.com` as SANs, which is the only reason the apex works: **a wildcard covers exactly one label and never the apex on its own.**
  - **A `Host:` HEADER OVER LOOPBACK IS ALSO HOW YOU TELL AN UNBOUND HOST FROM A MISCONFIGURED ONE**, and the discriminator is the RESPONSE SIZE, not the status code. An unbound host falls through to the IIS default site and answers **200 at ~703 bytes**; the real home page is **~5.4 MB**. So "200" proves nothing here — that is what made `investoyard.com` look served when nothing was bound for it. Over HTTPS use `curl -sk --resolve <host>:443:127.0.0.1`, which exercises the real SNI binding and the real certificate.
- **i18n** — `packages/i18n` en+hi (front-site feature pages are currently EN-literal; hindi pass pending). **THE SWITCHER IS HIDDEN ON THE WEB SINCE 2026-10-22 and the Hindi pages are NOT** — `/hi` still builds, still sits in the sitemap with its hreflang alternates, and `?lang=hi` still resolves. See *The language switcher is hidden, and only the switcher*; restoring the control is one commented line in `SiteHeader`.
- Round-based workflow: build → deploy → user verifies → "commit all". PowerShell 5.1: no `&&`, no embedded `"` in git -m (use quote-free here-strings).

## Subscription surface (2026-09-19 → 2026-09-23 rebuild)
- **Two surfaces, one panel set**: `/subscription` (`SubscriptionHub`) shows every open IPO as cards; `/subscription/v2` (`NseIpoLanding`, misnamed for legacy — it's the single-issue landing) shows ONE IPO based on `?symbol=X`. Both use `ShareWisePanel` + `AppWisePanel` + `LotLadderPanel` (exported from `SubscriptionHub.tsx`) so vocabulary and math never drift. Detail-page Live Subscription section (`LiveSubscription.tsx`) reuses the SAME panels — three surfaces, one source of truth.
  - **THAT CLAIM WAS HALF TRUE FOR MONTHS AND READ AS WHOLE, which is the more useful half of it** (corrected 2026-10-21). The PANELS were genuinely shared; **what fed them was not.** The detail page passed BAKED props while the other two re-fetched on mount, so the same component rendered a stale issue — and the summary strip above the panels was never shared at all: its Applications tile, its tone word and its tone COLOUR were each a second implementation. **A shared component is not a shared answer.** ~~Three things had to be added~~ **FOUR, and the fourth was found the NEXT DAY** (2026-10-22) — which is itself the lesson: a claim that a surface now agrees with another is worth re-testing rather than re-reading. The first three: `useLiveIpo()` so all three read one live record, `total.bidCount` as the one Applications expression, and one exported `tone()` / `toneLabel()`.
    - **THE FOURTH: THE AS-OF STAMP, WHERE ONE VARIABLE HELD TWO SHAPES.** `asOf` arrived from `IpoDetailView` **already formatted and date-only** (`fmtDate(raw.slice(0,10))`) while the live record’s `subscriptionAsOf` is a **raw ISO string**, and `LiveSubscription` prefers the live record and falls back to the prop. So which shape reached the page depended on which branch won — and **`useLiveIpo()` landing is what made the raw branch the normal one**, so the detail page began printing *"Updated 2026-10-22T09:48:09.015Z"* to customers where the hub prints *"Updated 22-Oct-2026 2:16 PM"* from the same field. **A fix that makes two surfaces read one record can expose a SECOND disagreement about how that record is rendered.**
    - Formatting moved INTO the component so both branches go through one formatter, rather than formatting one of them at the call site; the prop now carries the RAW value, which is what the hub’s own expression takes. `fmtDateTime` returns `""` for a missing or unparseable value, which the render already treated as absent, so no guard changed.
    - **TEST FOR THE SHAPE, NOT FOR AN EXPECTED STRING.** The check is `/\d{4}-\d{2}-\d{2}T/` against the rendered text, which cannot pass by coincidence the way comparing against one formatted string can. On the published build it reads false, and the only ISO left on the page is inside Next’s RSC script payload, which is not visible text.
- **`/subscription/v2` is dynamic**: reads `?symbol=X` from `useSearchParams`, falls back to NSE only when no param. Client-side `document.title` update — the static `metadata` export is generic. Initial `useState` seeds `null` when `baked.symbol !== requested` to prevent NSE flashing on VARMORA loads. `loaded` flag distinguishes "still fetching" (light placeholder) from "not in catalogue" (proper empty state). Never seed with the wrong IPO.
- **Click-through from hub cards**: each `IpoRow` in the hub renders the IPO name as a link + a small `→` icon-button next to the Share button → `/subscription/v2?symbol=X`. Same button on the Live Subscription card top-strip on the detail page. The arrow is behind `IpoRow`'s **`detailLink`** prop (default true) and `NseIpoLanding` passes **false** — it used to render unconditionally, so on `/subscription/v2` it sat right of the Applications tile pointing at the page the reader was already on.
- **Day-wise + hourly on `/subscription/v2` ONLY**: detail page carries only the summary card; the fuller day-wise (with grouped `NII → bHNI + sHNI` columns) + expandable hourly drill-down (chevron per day, click to expand `subLogHour` rows) lives on `/subscription/v2`. The `▸`/`▾` chevron only shows for days that have hourly points; older records show `·` and the row is not clickable.
- **…and as a MODAL on `/subscription`** (2026-09-23): a chart-icon button on each hub card opens the same `DayWiseSubscription` in the portal `Modal`, hourly drill-down intact, so the reader never leaves the page. **No fetch is added** — `subLog`/`subLogHour` already ride along on every IPO from `getIpos()`. The button renders only when the poller has actually written days (an empty modal is worse than no button), and `DayWiseSubscription` takes a `bare` prop that drops its `.panel` shell + heading, because the modal's title bar already says whose day-wise it is. `IpoRow` shows it only when the caller passes `onDayWise`, so `/subscription/v2` — which has the full table below the card — does not get it.
- **Hub order is CLOSE DATE, not `compareForList`** (operator ask, 2026-09-23): closing today, then tomorrow, then the day after, tie-broken by symbol; an IPO with no close date sorts last rather than first, which an empty string would otherwise invert. The cards' comparator now collapses its open stages the same way, so the two pages agree — **except** that the hub keeps its Mainboard/SME sections by operator choice, so an SME closing TODAY still renders below every Mainboard row. That is a known, accepted consequence, not a bug.
- **The card's action rail is a COLUMN** (`.subv2-ln-act`). The grid reserves a **44px** slot for it and the ≤1100px layout floats it over the top-right corner beside the `Updated …` stamp — both sized when there was ONE button. A third (day-wise) made a horizontal row 102px wide: it overflowed the 44px track on wide screens and ran **64px through the timestamp** on narrow ones. Stacked it is 34px wide and both layouts hold. Two things travel with it: `.subv2-ln-l` gets `padding-right` in the narrow layout so a long name can't slide under the floating rail, and `.subv2-updated`'s `right` is **74px** — what it takes to clear the rail in BOTH layouts, versus the 1px the arithmetic alone gives. **Adding a fourth button means re-measuring both layouts, not just looking at one.**
- **Book / Subscribed / Times cross-check**: `subscriptionTable(ipo)` in `ipoCalc.ts` computes `Book × Times = Subscribed` on every row. QIB Book Size is **NET** (post-anchor) — see the anchor rules below. Subscribed uses raw `nseShares + bseShares` from the poller (actual bid amount); Times = Subscribed / Book — recomputed locally so all three columns cross-check regardless of what the exchange sends. For QIB this yields a HIGHER Times than what NSE's own page shows (their times is against gross), reflecting truth against actually-available shares.
- **STORED times ≠ DISPLAYED times, and confusing them will cost you an afternoon.** The poller stores `demand ÷ GROSS offered` (`computeSubs` divides by `offeredFromIpo`, which does NOT deduct anchor); the page recomputes `demand ÷ NET book` for display. Both are deliberate. So a QIB row can store `0.04` and render `0.11×` and nothing is wrong. This is why `subscriptionTable`'s fallback for rows missing share columns MUST be `grossBook × times` — `netBook × times` undercounts by the anchor ratio, which is the 2026-09-21 report (₹12.77 Cr shown for an actual ₹31.97 Cr, exactly 2.5× at a 60% anchor). A 2026-09-23 attempt to "fix" the fallback to net was argued from the DISPLAYED figure and would have re-created that bug. **Settle it with data, not reasoning**: for any IPO with an anchor and real `nseShares`, compare the stored `timesSubscribed` against `demand/gross` and `demand/net` — on VARMORA · SWASTIKAIN · ADROITIND · ARMEE it matched GROSS every time.
- **Book Size drift against other information sites is our ROUNDED `issueSize`.** Cross-checked against yashasvifinvest 2026-09-23: **ARMEE and ELEVATE matched to the share** — their issue sizes are round (₹300.0000 Cr, ₹2100.0000 Cr) — while ADROITIND was +0.48% on QIB, SWASTIKAIN +0.19%, VARMORA −0.006%. Back-solving their share counts gives ₹150.7098 Cr, ₹160.8750 Cr and ₹708.0151 Cr against the ₹151 / ₹161 / ₹708 we store. Every bucket is `pct × issueSize ÷ price`, so the rounding lands on all four. **QIB reads worst** (0.48% vs retail's 0.19%) because the anchor subtracted from it is exact while the gross carries the error — the same absolute error over a much smaller net. The anchors themselves back-solved to exactly what the operator entered, so they are right. **RESOLVED 2026-09-24** — worst error across the five open issues is now **0.006%** (ADROITIND 0.478% → 0.004%, SWASTIKAIN 0.192% → 0.006%).
- **`Ipo.issueSize` (the COLUMN) and `extra.issueSizeCr` can desync, and only one of them is read by each caller.** After the operator corrected the three sizes, ADROITIND and SWASTIKAIN had `extra.issueSizeCr` = 150.71 / 160.88 while the column still said 151 / 161 — VARMORA's updated, the other two didn't. **CAUSE FOUND 2026-09-25, and it IS the Fresh/OFS-legs path** — plus a second bug beside it, see the next bullet. **Book Size was unaffected**, because `offeredByBucket()` reads the stored `sharesUpper` first and the form's Autofill had already recomputed those from the corrected figure — the column is only a fallback. **`scripts/normalise-reservation-shares.js` divides by the COLUMN**, though, so re-running it catalogue-wide would have silently overwritten the correction: measured at **+10,656 shares (0.189%) on ADROITIND** and 3,240 on SWASTIKAIN. Columns set to match `extra` on 2026-09-24. **When a figure exists in both places, check both before trusting either.**
- **Every LEGGED issue desynced by construction, and it published a wrong figure to customers** (fixed 2026-09-25). When both legs are entered, *Total issue size (₹ Cr)* goes **read-only** and renders `legSumCr` — so `form.issueSizeCr` keeps whatever was typed before that, usually the Fresh leg alone. `payload()` then sent that stale field as the COLUMN while `extra.issueSizeCr` took the leg sum. **ORIENTCABL opened 25 Sep publishing `Issue Size ₹320 Cr` against a ₹552 Cr offer**, its reservation legend reading `QIB 1.07 Cr sh · ₹160.00 Cr` — right shares, rupees off by 1.725× — while its own Issue-details panel said Fresh ₹320 Cr + OFS ₹232 Cr. Both now send the same expression. A catalogue scan found five more disagreeing by >0.5% and **all five have legs, 5 for 5**: MANIKA · MPIMANIPAL · PRANAV · DEEPA · MOMSBELIEF, all closed.
  - **Second bug beside it**: the read mapping did `Math.round(issueSize / 1e7)` for the admin edit field, so ₹150.7098 Cr loaded as `151` and the next save wrote `151` back — quietly undoing the operator's correction. That is why ADROITIND and SWASTIKAIN kept reverting. Now 4 dp on read, whole rupees on write.
  - **Fix the size, then re-run `backfill-resv-source.js`** — correcting ORIENTCABL's column brought its four rows inside tolerance and they stamped themselves. The script divides by the COLUMN, so a stale column makes honest rows look like nonsense.
- **TWO figures, TWO jobs: the OFFERED count ROUNDS, the ALLOCATION count FLOORS** (2026-09-24). Reservation share counts are what the exchange and every information site publish, and those are the raw percentage of the offer — `2,39,19,428` for VARMORA's QIB, which is not a multiple of its 101 lot. We floored everything, so every row came out short by under a lot (2 / 21 / 61 / 21) and *looked* like a bug to the operator comparing against a broker. `Math.round(pct × total)` reproduces all four broker figures **to the share**, so **Autofill and `normalise-reservation-shares.js` now round**. **`computeIssue()` still floors with residual absorption** — that is the spec's §9 ALLOCATION rule, because you cannot allot a fraction of a lot, and it is a different question from what the issue offered. Do not "unify" them.
- **…and it caught us a THIRD time, through the carve-out** (2026-09-26). With RUNWALENTR's employee reservation finally entered, *Net QIB after anchor* read `32,55,753` against the exchange's `32,55,739`. `netQibOffered` divided `netOfferShares` — the ALLOCATION net offer — and 1,20,275 is not a multiple of the 49 lot, so the carve-out floored to `1,20,246`, left **29 shares** in the net offer and put **14 of them in QIB**. It now divides `netOfferOffered` (carve-out verbatim, total rounded), and `computeScenario` carries **both bases side by side** rather than deriving one from the other. **`netQibOffered` had NO test**, which is how the same figure needed two operator reports — VARMORA's absorbed residual, then this; there are two now, built from the published table.
- **The same split reaches the ANCHOR panel, and it caught us there too.** *Net QIB after anchor* read the allocation figure — QIB floored to lot **plus the absorbed residual** — so VARMORA showed `95,67,755` where the exchange, the brokers and our own Book Size all say `95,67,653`; the whole 102-share gap is the 104-share residual QIB takes as the largest quota. `computeIssue()` now returns **`netQibOffered`** (`round(pct × net offer) − anchor`) beside `netQibShares`, and the form + `admin/catalog/view` render it. **ADDED, never replaced**: absorbing the residual is what makes the category shares sum to the net offer, which B01/B04 exist to guarantee — changing `netQibShares` would trade 102 shares for a broken post-condition. **`anchorMfShares` / `qibMfShares` keep dividing `netQibShares`** (operator ruling): a third of the anchor book to mutual funds is an allocation question, not a published one. **`anchorInsPensionShares` joins them 2026-09-30** — a SECOND reserved slice of the anchor book, **6.67% for domestic life insurers and pension funds**, which with the mutual-fund third accounts for 40% of the anchor portion. Same allocation basis, same floor-to-lot, rendered read-only beside the MF figure on the form. `anchorInsPensionPct` carries the same **`-- VERIFY`** caveat `anchorMfPct` already did: both are regulatory percentages living in SOURCE, not in the versioned seed config the brief asks for, so a SEBI amendment is still a deploy. So the panel deliberately shows two bases at once and the 102 shares will not reconcile if you subtract them — that is correct, not a bug.
- **`totalOfferedShares()` in `ipoCalc.ts` is the ONE resolver** — stated `extra.totalShares` first, `issueSize ÷ upper band` second — and `reservation()`, `offeredByBucket()`, `subscriptionTable()` and Autofill all call it. Until 2026-09-24 only `issueInputsFor()` honoured the stated count, so the moment an operator filled that field the admin form derived from 4,78,38,855 while the public page still derived from 4,78,39,155: a 300-share gap that surfaced as 303 on QIB. **The stated count is also the more accurate input** — it is what the exchanges publish and it involves no division to round away.
- **`Total issue size (shares)` DERIVES ITSELF now, and it fills rather than overwrites** (2026-09-25). `round(₹ total ÷ upper band)` — ₹90.17 Cr ÷ ₹167 = 53,99,401.1976 → **53,99,401**, SHAHINVEST's stated count to the share. **`extra.totalSharesSource`** marks it `derived` or `operator`, exactly as `shareResv[].source` does, and a typed count is never touched. **The reason is data, not caution: of the ten records holding both figures only TWO agree.** Overwriting would have turned VARMORA's 4,78,38,855 into 4,78,39,189 — the 300-share gap above, back again — and flattened ADROITIND's 1,12,47,000, round *because the document states it*, into 1,12,47,015.
  - **The field stays EDITABLE, unlike the ₹ box above it**, and that asymmetry is deliberate: legs → ₹ is addition, ₹ → shares is a division by a price out of a figure already rounded to two decimals in Cr. Read-only would make the RHP counts impossible to enter at all.
  - **The cascade**: when the total moves, reservation rows whose `source` is `derived` refresh themselves — our own snapshots, so nothing of the operator's is lost. `exchange` and `operator` rows keep their counts. 155 rows follow the total; the 22 unattributed ones do not.
  - **`offerTotals()` in `packages/shared-types/src/offerTotals.ts` is the ONE offer-total resolver.** Three disagreed whenever no count was stated: the public page used the raw quotient (53,99,401.1976), `computeIssue` floored it to a lot (53,99,370) and Autofill used the quotient again. Persisting the derived count is what makes all three divide one number, and `totalOfferedShares()` rounds its own fallback — **you cannot offer 0.1976 of a share**.
    - **It lived in `IpoForm.tsx` as `offerTotalsOf()`, where nothing could test it, and it carried a wrong assumption for exactly that reason** (moved + fixed 2026-09-30). The floor band was `net × cap ÷ floor`, commented *"the same MONEY buying cheaper shares"* — true only when every leg is priced in RUPEES. **A leg sized in SHARES is a COUNT and does not move with the price at all.** On a mainboard offer of Fresh ₹500 Cr + an OFS of 1,00,00,000 shares, band ₹190–200, it gave a floor-band net of `3,65,78,947` against a true `3,60,52,632` — **1.46% high, on every lower-band reservation count**. `computeIssue()` has always resolved each leg at the scenario price; only this resolver did not.
    - **A STATED total pins the CAP, and the floor moves by the DELTA the legs imply** — not by a price ratio. That one expression covers all three cases: every leg in shares → delta 0, the count does not move; every leg in ₹ → exactly the old price scaling; mixed → only the ₹ portion moves.
    - **Measured across the catalogue before shipping: the CAP is unchanged on all 46 records with a usable total**, so nothing published moved, and 31 are identical at the floor too. The 15 that move split exactly as the theory predicts — **4 share-sized records by 5.4–6.5%** (PIND · SHREETNB · SAIURJA · SPECTRAA, the full width of the band) and **11 ₹-sized by ≤0.046%**, which is the carve-out now resolving at the floor's OWN price instead of being scaled from the cap's.
    - **No stored data was corrupted**: all four share-sized records turned out to have no reservation counts written at all (SPECTRAA's are `operator`), so the fault was latent, waiting for the first Autofill. **A latent bug in a resolver nothing can test is the argument for moving it, not for leaving it.** 13 tests, built from the mainboard worked example at both bands plus the RUNWALENTR published figures.
    - `scripts/refill-derived-reservation.js` imports the same function now; it had a COPY, which is precisely how a wrong floor band survives in two places.
- **A carve-out comes OFF THE TOP, and every percentage below is a percentage of what is LEFT** (2026-09-25). Chittorgarh states it outright for RUNWALENTR: total `1,63,98,962`, employee `1,20,275`, **net offer `1,62,78,687`**, and QIB's `81,39,344` is **50% of the NET** — 49.63% of the total. `computeIssue()` always modelled this (spec §2.5, Step 2); the admin form's row derivation did not, so it divided the GROSS and put **57,378 shares too many into QIB on a live issue**. `offerTotalsOf()` now subtracts the carve-out first.
  - **Carve-outs take SHARES or ₹ Cr**, chosen per field. The basis was hardcoded to `amount` until this date, which made RUNWALENTR's reservation impossible to enter at all: the RHP states a count, and `1,20,275` against a lot of 49 is **not lot-aligned**, so no rupee figure reproduces it. Stored in `extra.carveoutBasis` — a SEPARATE key, because three live records and the Excel importer already write the flat `extra.carveouts` shape.
  - **A ₹ carve-out converts at its OWN price, not the band price.** Employees and shareholders bid at a discount, so `₹3.50 Cr ÷ ₹305` is 1,14,754 against a real 1,20,275 — **5,521 shares short, every one of which falls through into QIB**. The engine now divides by `price − discount` (falling back to `price` if the discount would take it to zero), and a test asserts it.
  - **The discount also changes the TOTAL share count**, which is why `derivedTotalSharesOf()` cannot simply divide: ₹500 Cr at ₹305 is 1,63,93,442, but with 1,20,275 shares sold at ₹291 the offer is **1,63,98,963**. Take the carve-out's rupees out, divide the rest at the band price, add its shares back.
  - **Verified against Chittorgarh**: QIB / bNII / sNII reproduce **exactly** from ₹500 Cr alone, with total / net / retail one share out — a single rounding step in reconstructing a total from an "up to ₹500 Cr". Type the published `1,63,98,962` into *Total issue size (shares)* and **all six are exact**. That is what that field is for.
  - **SHAREHOLDER works exactly the same way** (operator, 2026-09-28) — same carve-out, same read-only row, same exclusion from the split. `CARVEOUT_ROWS` is the one list both read, so neither can drift from the other. The market maker is deliberately NOT in it: an SME-only reservation that was never one of the seven categories and has no row to make read-only.
  - **POLICYHOLDER is a FOURTH carve-out, and adding it fixed a live mis-mapping** (2026-09-30). It is not the shareholder quota: a shareholder reservation answers to holders of a **listed parent**, a policyholder reservation to the insurer's **own policyholders**, and an issuer can offer both at once. The poller mapped the exchanges' **`POL` / `POLRET`** codes onto `shareholder` in BOTH `bucketNse` and `bucketBse`, so on such an issue two categories' demand summed into one row and was measured against a shareholder-only Book Size. **LICI proves it was live, not hypothetical** — it has no listed parent and so no shareholder quota, yet it carries a stored `shareholder` subscription row at **6.12×**, which is LIC's POLICYHOLDER figure under the wrong name.
    - It carries **its own discount** (`extra.policyholderDiscount`). LIC gave policyholders ₹60 and employees ₹45 on the same issue — two different discounts, which is precisely why it cannot borrow the shareholder field.
    - **Deliberately NOT extended to the bid/apply rail** — `ApplyWizard`, `bidEngine`, the print flow and allotment matching still know only employee/shareholder. A customer cannot apply under a policyholder quota through us, and adding the category there without the rail work would be a half-built path. This change is the OFFER side: carve-out, net offer, reservation row, Book Size, subscription label.
    - **LICI's stored row was relabelled on operator instruction** (2026-09-30), after its whole set was matched against LIC's published final subscription: QIB 2.83 · NII 2.91 · Retail 1.99 · Employee 4.40 · **6.12** · Total 2.95. LIC has no listed parent and so no shareholder quota — that 6.12× is the policyholder figure. The cross-check that settles it: **the 10 shareholder rows left in the catalogue are ALL subsidiaries of a listed parent** (AEROFLEX · AWL · BAJAJHFL · BLSE · HDFCAMC · HDFCLIFE · ICICIGI · SBILIFE · TATATECH · UJJIVANSFB), and LICI was the only one that was not.
      - **It needed no web rebuild, and that is worth knowing.** LICI is one of the ~250 records with no page in the static export (1,005 of 1,260 build), so it is served by the **`/ipos/live` client fallback** — which fetches from the API at request time. Verified in the browser: the page reads *Policyholder … 6.12×* with no Shareholder row. **A data fix on an older record reaches readers immediately; one on a BUILT page needs the rebuild.**
      - Its Book Size still shows 0 because LICI has no reservation table at all — the existing operator-data gap, not this change.
    - 7 tests, built from LIC's real reservation (2,21,37,492 policyholder + 15,81,249 employee against a 22,13,74,920-share offer).
  - **Generalising it exposed a latent double-count.** The employee row rendered `carveoutAt()`, which sums ALL carve-outs — invisible while employee was the only one set, and it would have shown the employee row the shareholder's shares the moment both were filled. `carveoutOneAt(f, key, price)` is per-key now and `carveoutAt` folds over it.
  - **The Employee ROW in the reservation table is now READ-ONLY**, derived from the carve-out and labelled *from carve-out*, and its `pct` is excluded from the split. Offering both was a trap: **AUGMONT** sums to 100.691% because the 0.691% employee quota was typed there and QIB silently absorbed the overshoot. A legacy record with a quota in the table and an empty carve-out now raises **E-RESV**, blocking — AUGMONT (0.691%) and **NSE** (43,33,437 shares) are the two, and both need the figure moved rather than left to evaporate.
- **The reservation Total row must sum the column it sits under — and it took TWO goes** (2026-09-25, then 2026-09-30). The second fault was subtler than the first: it re-derived every row from its PERCENTAGE rather than summing what the cell displays, so editing a **share count** left the cell showing the new figure while the Total went on adding the old derived one. Editing a **percentage** always worked, which is exactly why it read as intermittent. `shownCount(key, field, total)` is now the one resolver — stored count first, derivation second — and both the row and the Total call it, so they cannot drift again.
  - **The Total sums the SPLIT rows only and is labelled `Total (net offer)`** (operator's call, 2026-09-30). The carve-out rows are shown in the table but excluded, because ICDR takes a preferential reservation off the top BEFORE the split: including them would give a figure the percentages above cannot reconcile to 100% of. The label states the exclusion so it reads as deliberate rather than as an omission — they had been contributing 0 silently, which is the same bug wearing different clothes.
  - **What the FIRST fix was about, 2026-09-25**: it read `computeIssue`'s scenarios, which hold a stated share count fixed across BOTH price scenarios — correct for an offer quoted in shares — which at the time there was not one of, ~~all 35 fresh and 23 OFS legs being quoted in RUPEES~~ **STALE: nine SME records are share-quoted as of 2026-09-30, see the one-column rule below** — so on a ₹-quoted offer the count genuinely moves with the price. So the two totals printed the same number while the Lower column beneath them summed to something else — VARMORA showed `4,78,38,855` over rows adding to `5,05,72,503`. It appeared the day `totalShares` began auto-filling, because until then most records had no stated count and the two totals differed honestly. **A total that does not equal its own column is a bug whichever model is right.**
- **The drift marker was firing on clean records, and it was the round-vs-floor split again.** The reservation table compared a stored **offered** count against `computeIssue`'s **allocation** figure — floored to a lot with the residual absorbed by the largest quota. The residual exceeds a lot, so a record that had just been refilled perfectly still showed **±102 on VARMORA's QIB and ±100 on SHAHINVEST's**. Both sides are the offered figure now; `derivedRow()` still reads the allocation figure, because applications-for-1× **is** an allocation question. **Comparing the two bases can only ever mislead — check which one a figure is before you diff it.**
- **THE ANCHOR IS NOT A ROW IN THE RESERVATION TABLE, AND ADDING ONE WOULD DOUBLE-COUNT** (operator asked for exactly that on 2026-10-10; this is the answer and the reasoning is worth keeping). The four carve-outs come OFF THE TOP and are mutually exclusive with the split; the anchor is a **SUB-ALLOCATION OF QIB**, so QIB's count already contains it.
  - **THE TRAP IS THAT ON A BROKEN RECORD THE WRONG ANSWER LOOKS RIGHT.** LOANFRONT's table read **71.78%** and its anchor is **28.22%** of the issue, so adding an anchor row would have produced exactly **100%** — because TWO ERRORS CANCEL. On a correctly entered record the same row gives **128%**. The table was short because its QIB row held the post-anchor figure, not because the anchor was missing from it.
  - **QIB CARRIES TWO SUB-ROWS INSTEAD** — *Anchor portion* and *QIB (Ex. Anchor)*, chipped **within QIB** (operator asked for row-level lines on 2026-10-10, having first been given a single sub-line). **Sub-rows of QIB, never siblings of it**: QIB's count IS the anchor plus the ex-anchor, so three rows all counting toward the total would treble that quota. They are excluded from both Total rows, the same way the Share-wise panel never sums its NII subtotal alongside bHNI and sHNI.
  - **They sit on the PARENT's basis — percentages of the NET offer — so they visibly add up to the row above**: LOANFRONT reads QIB 49.88% over 29.71 + 20.17, and 22,00,000 + 14,94,000 = 36,94,000. **The anchor's share OF QIB (59.56%, the figure B09 caps) is in the TOOLTIP rather than the column**, because that column already carries two bases — split rows against the net offer, carve-out rows against the whole issue — and a third would be the "row disagreeing with itself" fault again.
  - Hidden when the anchor is absent or EXCEEDS QIB: the latter is the broken state W-ANCHOR is already explaining, and an ex-anchor of minus seven lakh shares explains nothing.
- **THE SUBSCRIPTION SURFACES NOW SAY QIB EXCLUDES THE ANCHOR — all three, from ONE place** (operator ask, 2026-10-10). `subscriptionTable` has deducted it since 2026-09-21 and the DETAIL page explained it in a footnote of its own, but **`/subscription` and `/subscription/v2` rendered the same deducted number and said nothing**, so a reader comparing our QIB against the issue's reservation table found it short by the anchor with no explanation on the page.
  - The note lives in **`ShareWisePanel`**, which IS all three surfaces, and `LiveSubscription` no longer repeats it — it would otherwise print twice on the detail page. Its own `lsx-note` keeps the band and Book × Times sentences.
  - **TWO WAYS OF SAYING IT, deliberately**: the row LABEL reads **`QIB (Ex. Anchor)`** so the number explains itself where it is read, and the footnote names the share count and which of the three states produced it. The label appears ONLY when a deduction was applied — on a record with no anchor figure the Book Size IS gross and the label would lie.
  - **This is NOT the same basis as the reservation legend, and that is correct rather than a disagreement**: the legend shows the FULL QIB quota, this shows what is left for the public book. Labelling this one *(Ex. Anchor)* is what lets a reader reconcile the two.
  - **Mobile does not carry it** — the Estimated-vs-Actual footnote was already a web-only parity gap and still is.
- **`DemandPanel` — Subscription Demand in ₹ CRORE, the third card** (operator ask, 2026-10-13, against ipopremium.in/view/subscription). Same four columns as Share-wise, valued in rupees rather than counted in shares. **NO NEW ARITHMETIC**: `subscriptionTable` already returns book size, subscribed and each row's own price, and Share-wise already printed the ₹ Cr under every share figure — this card promotes it to the headline.
  - **TIMES IS TAKEN FROM `r.times`, NEVER RECOMPUTED FROM THE ₹ FIGURES.** The ratio is identical either way, because both sides scale by the same price, so recomputing could only add rounding drift between two cards describing one issue. One renderer, or two renderers will disagree.
  - **Each row at ITS OWN price**, the same rule Share-wise follows — RUNWALENTR's employee row renders **₹3.50 Cr at ₹291**, not ₹3.67 at the ₹305 band, which is the discount trap this file has documented since 2026-09-28. Verified on the BUILT page, not just in the computation.
  - **The reference's QIB sub-rows (FIIs · DIIs · Mutual funds · Others) are not reproduced TODAY — but the claim recorded here that *"that breakdown is not in our data at all"* was WRONG, corrected 2026-10-13.** **BOTH exchanges send it and the poller discards it at two lines**: `bucketBse` collapses `QIB/IC/FI/FII/OTH/MF` into `qib` at the CATEGORY level (line 74), and `bucketNse(r.category)` is **not even passed the `subCategory` field** (line 128) that `nse-query.adapter.ts` has always parsed (`subCategory?: string; // IC / FII / MF / IND / CO …`, line 30, with a test). So this is a poller + display change and **NO schema change** — `IpoSubscription.category` is a free string, and a poller-owned `extra` key is the safer home anyway. **Before saying a figure is unavailable, grep the adapter for the field.**
    - **`scripts/probe-nse-catwise.js` is the tool** (read-only, one GET per symbol, no args = everything open today). It prints the raw rows WITH the four-line mapping and **cross-checks the sub-sum against our stored `qib` row**, so it answers the double-count question by itself rather than leaving it to be re-reasoned.
    - **PROBED THE LIVE FEED 2026-10-13 AND THE ANSWER IS CLEAN: NSE SENDS SUB-ROWS ONLY, WITH NO PARENT `QIB` ROW.** HDFIRE, mid-bidding: `FII 52,47,165` · `FI 770` · `IC 3,740` · `MF 2,310` · `OTH 71,665`, bare-QIB rows **0**, and their sum is **53,25,650 — EXACTLY the `qib` nseShares we already store.** So the breakdown is a **PARTITION of a total we already hold**, not a second opinion on it: nothing to add, nothing to subtract, and **no double-count risk**, which was the one question that could have made this expensive. `bidCount` arrives per sub-category too, so the Application-wise side is available if ever wanted.
    - **The five exchange codes map onto the reference's FOUR lines** — `FII → FIIs` · `MF → Mutual funds` · **`FI + IC → DIIs`** (financial institutions + insurance companies) · `OTH + anything unrecognised → Others`. That is why ipopremium prints four where the exchange sends five.
    - **ONLY NSE WAS PROBED.** BSE ships the same five as TOP-LEVEL categories (that is the line-74 collapse) but its live shape is **unverified — confirm it rather than assuming it matches**, the same standing caution as BSE's `ipoupistatus` codes.
    - **HISTORY CANNOT BE BACKFILLED**, exactly as with the `apps` key: every closed issue will never carry this, and so will the part of a live day that precedes the pool restart. **Deploy inside the 10:00–18:00 window** or lose the day — the 18:00 deploy that wasted a day is written up under the poller.
    - Plan agreed with the operator 2026-10-13, to build **2026-10-14**: store it as a poller-owned **`extra.qibSplit`** key added to `SERVER_OWNED_EXTRA`, **NOT as new `IpoSubscription` rows** — a sub-row has no reserved quota, so `computeSubs` would drop it at `if (offered <= 0) continue`, and four new categories would otherwise surface in `subscriptionTable`, Share-wise, Application-wise, day-wise AND the reservation legend, i.e. five surfaces to guard instead of none.
  - ~~**Layout**: column 2 STACKS Application-wise over the Lot ladder~~ **SUPERSEDED, TWICE.** On 2026-10-22 the ladder moved OUT into a full-width footer and `.subv2-det-stack` went with it; on 2026-10-23 the operator asked for it back under Application-wise, and it is there by GRID PLACEMENT (`grid-column: 2; grid-row: 2`) rather than by restoring the wrapper — so it looks as it did before and still sits LAST in the DOM. **The wrapper is gone for good; the position came back.** See the review section. The detail page has no Lot ladder, so there the card simply takes the third column. Still three grid columns on both.
  - Known nit, PRE-EXISTING: `LiveSubscription`'s footnote says *"Amounts at the upper band (₹305)"* while a discounted quota in either card is valued at its own price. It was already inaccurate beside Share-wise; this card makes it more visible.
- **Anchor deduction — three sources, in order**: `extra.anchorShares` → the **roster sum** (`extra.anchors[].shares`) → `extra.anchorPct × grossQIB / 100`. None present = silent no-op, gross QIB, never an assumed 60%. `subscriptionTable` returns **`anchorDeducted`** plus **`anchorBasis`** (`'actual' | 'estimated' | null`), and `LiveSubscription` prints one of **THREE** lines off them. That line used to print "Net" unconditionally and was **FALSE on five of the six IPOs open on 2026-09-23** — only VARMORA had an anchor figure. One figure can be checked against the exchange and the other cannot; say which.
  - **Estimated ≠ Actual, and the page says so since 2026-09-30.** A deduction taken from a stated share count (or a fully-priced roster) is the ALLOTMENT off the intimation letter. One taken from `anchorPct` is our own arithmetic against a ceiling **the issue may not have used** — an anchor book can close under 60%, so an estimate is an upper bound, not a figure. Census across the built export: **94 Gross · 34 actual · 4 ESTIMATED** (re-measured 30 Sep; it was 98 / 30 / 6 when the footnote shipped that morning, and SAIURJA plus the records repaired that day moved between buckets) — and those 6, **NSE among them**, were publishing *Net (post anchor)* in wording that read as the allotment.
- **The roster is read because the IPO form only CROSS-CHECKS it, never sums it in.** An operator who typed all fourteen investor rows off the intimation letter and left the total blank had a record that looked complete and still published a gross QIB (MPIMANIPAL). `anchorRosterShares()` returns **0 unless EVERY named investor carries a share count** — a half-typed roster would otherwise sum to something that looks like an allocation, deduct too little, and be wrong in a way nothing on the page could reveal.
- **`Anchor Investors` master ≠ per-IPO anchor allocation.** The master is a global list of investor NAMES for the picker (503 rows); nothing reads it for Book Size. The figure that matters is on the IPO form's Offer & Reservation tab. Confusing the two is why "I already added anchor data" and an empty `extra.anchorShares` can both be true at once. **`anchorDate` is not it either** — an operator filled that on five records and the QIB stayed gross; only `anchorShares` / `anchorPct` / a fully-priced roster feed the deduction.
- **The Anchor Intimation review can now ADD unmatched investors to the master** (`AnchorReviewModal`, 2026-09-23): a bulk `Add N to master` button plus a per-row clickable badge; both `createMaster('anchors')`, re-resolve and flip the row to `✓ master` before Fill. Until then the badge only REPORTED the mismatch — the whole modal had two click handlers, Cancel and Fill — and unmatched rows applied as free text, which is how one fund becomes two entities across reports. Two things it handles on purpose: a **duplicate is not an error** (the API throws `P2002` → *"That name already exists"* when the name IS in the master but the parser didn't resolve it; the catch re-fetches and links it), and what gets created is the letter's **RAW** name, the one on screen — the parser mangles fund names (`IDPITER` for `JUPITER`) and a bad row is far easier to avoid than to undo once it is in the master and mapped.
- **The reservation table shows ONE share-count column when the offer is sized in SHARES** (2026-09-29). A share-sized offer reserves the same count whichever price the book strikes, so upper and lower would print the identical figure twice and imply a difference that does not exist. Derived from the **Fresh / OFS leg basis**, not a new "sized by" control — the operator already states it there and a second switch for one fact is a second thing that can disagree. **Every priced leg must be `shares`**: a mixed offer still moves with price and keeps both columns. **EVERY SHARE-SIZED RECORD IS SME, and THE COUNT IN THIS NOTE ROTS — re-measure rather than trusting it.** It read "one", then "four", then **NINE** (2026-09-30: SAIURJA · SPECTRAA · EIMS · BLACKOPAL with both legs in shares, plus DUDANI · PIND · SHREETNB · SHIVCHEM · ACMEUNIV on a lone fresh leg — the rule fires on a SINGLE `shares` leg). By 2026-10-09 **AXIOMGAS · ROBOKIDZ · BMISL · HIMALAYAN · ACMERAIL · TNA qualified too** — every SME record entered since. The RULE is stable; the census is a snapshot. **The board is the pattern: every share-sized record is SME and every mainboard record is rupee-sized** — so a mainboard page keeps both columns and an SME one may not. **NOT ONE record is MIXED**, which is why Phase A's 1.46% mixed-basis case stays hypothetical while its share-sized half was real. Do not assume a ₹ leg when reading this code.
- **The percentage cell goes READ-ONLY when the stored count is authoritative** — `source: exchange | operator` — and shows what that count actually works out to, chipped *from count*. An editable round number beside a document figure invites the operator to "correct" the percentage and silently disagree with the RHP. A `derived` count came FROM the percentage, so there the percentage stays the input.
- **An all-derived reservation table carries an "Estimated — verify with the RHP" banner.** A percentage split reproduces an RHP only when the issuer used round numbers: **Sai Urja's RHP puts QIB at 9,79,200 where 50% of its net offer is 9,96,000**, because each category is rounded to whole applications and then adjusted at the BRLM's discretion. Nothing derived is wrong — it just is not the document, and the banner is the difference. It names how many of the filled counts are derived, and disappears once they all carry `exchange` or `operator` provenance.
- **`minApplicationShares()` in `packages/shared-types` is the ONE minimum-bid definition** (2026-09-29) — read by `lotLadder()`, `subscriptionTable()`'s Req 1× and the card's `formsFor1x`. Three copies existed and **two were wrong**: both handed the **₹2 L** divisor to the bHNI band as well as sHNI, so *"applications required for 1×"* ran **~4.8× high on every issue** — 29 mainboard and 13 SME (ORIENTCABL B-HNI read 2,636 against a true 551; NSE read 1,05,331 against 22,253). On SME it was worse: retail divided by ONE lot where the minimum is two (ROBOKIDZ 797 against 399), and sHNI could land on the same lot count as the individual bid, making the two indistinguishable. **`lotLadder()` had it right the whole time and nothing compared them** — which is the argument for the shared helper, not the individual fixes. 10 tests, built from a real RHP (Sai Urja Indo Ventures, BSE SME: 2 / 3 / 8 lots → 296 / 28 / 21 forms).
  - **SME and mainboard differ in KIND.** An SME individual bids a FIXED two lots, so sHNI must start one lot ABOVE it — deriving sHNI from ₹2 L alone collides with retail. Mainboard retail is a RANGE (1 lot up to ₹2 L), so sHNI begins one lot past whatever fits under ₹2 L. Both then place bHNI one lot past ₹10 L.
- **Req 1× — SEBI rules only where they exist**: Retail = Book ÷ lotSize (1-lot min for mainboard, **2-lot min for SME** per post-2024 amendment). HNI/HNI2 = Book ÷ 120 (₹2L min bid → `ceil(200_000 / (lot × priceMax)) × lot`). **Other = 0** — not a real category, so it must not be guessed. Render `0` literally, not `—`.
  - ~~**Employee / Shareholder = 0, and excluded from Application-wise**~~ **REVERSED 2026-09-30, and settled with DATA rather than argument.** The old rule said these have "no standard min-bid rule", which made `Req 1×` print 0 and was the whole reason they left the Application-wise view on 2026-09-28. **The demand disagrees: every employee row carrying real share figures is an EXACT multiple of the lot — 4 of 4, with retail 20 of 20 as the control** — so employees bid in whole lots and the minimum is at least one. **And it is exactly one, not two: three of those four (NSE · NITYAS · RUNWALENTR) are NOT multiples of two lots**, which a two-lot minimum could not produce. Average bid runs 1.47–7.80 lots. `minApplicationLots` now returns **1** for employee · shareholder · policyholder, and `APP_WISE_ORDER` carries all three again.
  - **ONLY MAINBOARD IS EVIDENCED.** All four rows are mainboard and no SME issue in the catalogue carries a reserved quota, so SME takes one lot as the conservative floor — no bid can be smaller — rather than inheriting the 2-lot individual minimum. Revisit if an SME issue with a reserved quota appears.
  - **The customer-visible change is THREE pages, not 134.** 134 rows gain a real minimum, but only **NITYAS (500) · AONESTEELS (1,473) · RUNWALENTR (2,455)** gain a figure — the other 131 have no employee reservation on the record at all, so they still show `—`. That is the existing operator-data gap, not a regression.
- **A preferential reservation reaches the subscription surfaces through `carveoutAt()`** (`packages/shared-types/src/carveouts.ts`, 2026-09-28) — employee AND shareholder, on either basis. A carve-out lives in `extra.carveouts`, never in `extra.shareResv`, so `offeredFromIpo()` (poller) and `offeredByBucket()` (web) gave it no Book Size — and `computeSubs` then **threw the exchange's demand away** at `if (offered <= 0) continue`. RUNWALENTR was receiving **49,294 shares of real employee demand every poll** and discarding all of it; the category was not hidden, it was never computed. A typed `shareResv` count still wins; carve-outs only fill what that table leaves empty.
  - **Every Share-wise row is valued at ITS OWN price** — the band price less that category's discount, carried as `SubRowT.price` so all display sites agree (2026-09-28). A discounted quota valued at the band price reads high: 1,20,275 employee shares are ₹3.50 Cr at ₹291, not ₹3.67 Cr at ₹305. Only `employeeDiscount` / `shareholderDiscount` / `retailDiscount` exist; every other category falls back to the band price, which is why this is safe to apply to all rows. **The code can only apply a discount that is ENTERED**, and almost none are: only a handful carry one — FIVE, listed below; this clause originally said "four" while naming three (RENTOMOJO ₹20 · NSE ₹170 · **RUNWALENTR ₹14, set 2026-09-28** → its employee book reads ₹3.50 Cr). ~~**AONESTEELS** and **NITYAS**~~ **BOTH DONE 2026-09-30.** AONESTEELS carries `employeeDiscount: 38` (band ₹385–405), so its 54,496 employee shares value at ₹367. **NITYAS carries `7`** (band ₹70–75) — confirmed against two independent sources whose employee reservation of **1,00,000 shares matched our carve-out to the share** — so its employee book reads **₹0.68 Cr** instead of ₹0.75 Cr. **FIVE records carry an employee discount, not six** (counted from the database 2026-09-30, after I had written "six"): **AONESTEELS ₹38 · NITYAS ₹7 · NSE ₹170 · RENTOMOJO ₹20 · RUNWALENTR ₹14**. My arithmetic was right and its BASE was not — the note above said "four records" while naming only three, so every count built on it was one high. **Counting a list is cheaper than trusting a tally beside it.**
      - **AUGMONT now has an employee carve-out (2,76,400) and NO discount**, so those shares value at the full ₹788 band price. That is a consequence of moving its quota out of `shareResv` on 2026-09-30 — the carve-out did not exist when the discount sweep was done, so "no carve-out is left valuing at the band price" stopped being true the moment I created one. **Check AUGMONT's RHP.**
      - **NITYAS was LIVE when it was written** (opened 30 Sep), which is the case the jsonb merge exists for: 62 `extra` keys before and after, `subLogHour` still 5 buckets, and the value **survived a poll 9 seconds later**. A read-modify-write would have taken whatever the poller wrote in between. Re-read after writing to a live record — a script reporting success has only proved it issued the write. The field is **Add IPO → Pricing tab → "Discounts" panel → `Employee (₹)`**, beside Retail and Shareholder.
- **SME lot ladder**: `lotLadder(ipo)` returns 3 rows for SME (Individual @ 2 lots, sHNI, bHNI) vs 5 rows for mainboard (with min-max ranges). SME variant of `LotLadderPanel` renders `CATEGORY | LOT(S) | QTY | AMOUNT | RESERVED` — the Reserved column is Req 1× from `subscriptionTable`. Applies to both `/subscription/v2` hub cards and the detail-page LiveSubscription.
- ~~**`AppWisePanel` is hidden for SME** (redundant with the Reserved column)~~ **REVERSED 2026-10-09 — SME SHOWS IT, and the old reasoning was true of ONE COLUMN OUT OF FOUR.** The panel is `Category | Req 1× | Total | Times`. Reserved **is** `req1x`, so that column genuinely did repeat — but **Total (applications RECEIVED) and Times (the by-applications multiple) appeared nowhere on an SME page.** SAIURJA shows it plainly: Retail Req 1× **296** equals the ladder's Reserved **296**, while **1,712 applications and 5.78×** were invisible. **A redundancy claim has to be checked column by column**; "it's the same number" was right about the one figure anyone had looked at.
  - **Three mounts, all unhidden**: `IpoRow` in `SubscriptionHub.tsx` (which serves BOTH the hub cards and `/subscription/v2`, so they are one code path), `LiveSubscription.tsx` for the detail page, and `DayWiseSubscription`'s Share/Application toggle, whose `sme` prop existed only to follow the panel and is **deleted** along with both call sites.
  - **Safe unconditionally** — `AppWisePanel` returns `null` when no row carries a bid count, so an SME issue the exchange gives no application figures for renders nothing rather than an empty table. **4 of the 9 SME issues with subscription rows carry them** (ROBOKIDZ · SPECTRAA · SAIURJA · RKFAL); only **RKFAL** has them in its day-wise log, since application logging began 2026-10-01 and cannot be backfilled.
  - **The grid was already built for three panels** — `.subv2-det-in` has three tracks (described here as `2fr 1fr 1fr`, which it never literally was — see the review section for what it actually holds), so SME had been rendering two into it and leaving the third column empty. No CSS change; the fix fills the layout as designed.
- **A PRO-RATA QUOTA HAS NO APPLICATION-WISE MULTIPLE — Employee / Shareholder / Policyholder read `NA` and `—`** (operator ask, 2026-10-21). `Req 1×` and `Times` answer *"what are my chances"*, which is a question about a **DRAW**. Retail is drawn — minimum lot to as many as it reaches, then a draw of lots — so there the application count is the inverse of a chance. The reserved quotas are allotted **PROPORTIONATELY**: the SHARE-wise multiple decides an allotment and the application count decides nothing.
  - **JPL made it visible.** Its employee quota is OVER-subscribed at **1.08× by shares** — every applicant gets roughly 93% of their bid — while this table read **0.16×** in a low-demand tone. Two panels on one screen pointing opposite ways, with the colour on the misleading one. Shareholder read 1.01× against 0.21×.
  - **THE FIGURE WAS NOT WRONG, which is the subtle part**: `book ÷ one lot` is a true capacity count. It is the FRAME that does not apply, so the honest rendering is *not applicable* rather than a corrected number — and **the row keeps its application COUNT**, the one figure on it that means something. Dropping the rows is what the 2026-09-28 state did, and it lost exactly that.
  - **`NA` IS NOT A DASH.** It follows the `na` check state in `ipo-completeness`: *"not applicable"* is a different statement from *"not known"*, and a dash in both cells reads as a gap somebody forgot to fill. The tone class goes with the number — a dash has no magnitude to colour.
  - **BOTH NII BANDS ARE PROPORTIONATE UNDER ICDR TOO and are deliberately NOT in the list** (operator ruling — a strict reading would take them and leave Retail alone). NII has a HARD minimum allotment parcel — the ₹2 L application size, with a draw of lots when the quota cannot give everyone that much — so its count is a real capacity figure, which is what `minAllotmentLots` was built for the day before. **Worth knowing before anyone unifies them: on JPL bHNI's gap between the two multiples (0.70× against 0.08×) is WIDER than employee's, and correct there**, because a bHNI must bid over ₹10 L while the parcel is ₹2 L worth.
  - `PRO_RATA_CATEGORIES` / `isProRataAllotment()` live in `appWise.ts`; `subscriptionTable` applies it ONCE and sets **`SubRowT.proRata`**, leaving `req1x` and `applicationsTimes` genuinely UNSET rather than zeroed — a value nothing may print should not be on the row, or it leaks into the fourth surface later. `appWiseRows` admits a pro-rata row on its bid count alone.
  - **THE SHARE SNAPSHOT KEPT A FOURTH HAND-WRITTEN COPY OF THE ROW FILTER, and it had already drifted** — it named `employee` but neither `shareholder` nor `policyholder`, so **every shared JPL image had been dropping a quota**. Nobody reported it; it surfaced only because this change broke the copy's `applicationsTimes != null` test, which would have made Employee vanish from the image entirely. It reads `appWiseRows` now. **The `appWise.ts` docblock warned that a fourth copy is how a category goes missing, and a fourth copy already existed.**
  - **The poller is UNTOUCHED.** Unlike the QIB multiple — which divided an institutional bid by one retail lot and was arithmetically garbage — this figure is true and only its frame is wrong, so `applicationsSubscribed` keeps being stored and a reversal is a display change. **The day-wise table is unaffected**: its columns are QIB / bHNI / sHNI / Retail / Total only, so these categories were never there.
  - Measured before shipping: **10 issues carry these figures and JPL is the only one still open**; no issue has pro-rata rows alone, so no panel gains or loses its visibility.
- **THE DETAIL PAGE'S DOCUMENTS PANEL WAS TRAPPED INSIDE `section#about`** (reported as "RHP/DRHP missing on SME", fixed 2026-10-09). That section's gate is the company description / strengths / contact info, so on any record where the operator had not written that prose the panel **never rendered even though the documents were there** — measured at **469 records with public documents nobody could see: 298 MAINBOARD, 171 SME**, against 40 that rendered. **It is not an SME bug**; SME simply rarely carries the rich About content, and in absolute terms mainboard was hiding twice as many. The SME-norms panel was in the same section with the same fate. Both are now top-level sections gated on their OWN data, plus a Documents tab entry. **A panel must never depend on another panel's content being present** — grep for what encloses a section before concluding its own condition is at fault.
  - **`docLabel()` / `isPublicDoc()` in `packages/shared-types/src/ipoDocs.ts` are the ONE resolvers** (9 tests), because **both surfaces got this wrong in different ways**: web printed `d.type` raw, so a row stored `"rhp"` read lowercase beside one stored `"RHP"` from the form; **mobile kept a FOUR-ENTRY label map and used it as the FILTER**, so a document typed `Financials` or `Other` was dropped from the page entirely. That one is **latent** — the data holds only `rhp` / `RHP` / `DRHP` today — but it waits for the first other type. An unknown type is now **labelled, never dropped**: the operator attached it deliberately.
  - **"Open PDF" was wrong 537 times out of 543.** Of the 543 public documents, **468 are `.zip`** (NSE archives serve RHPs zipped), 69 are `.html`, and **only 6 are actually PDFs**. Mobile said "Open PDF ›"; both surfaces now say "Open ↗". **Do not name a file format in a link label unless something guarantees it.**
  - `key={d.type}` dropped a document whenever a record carried two of one type.
- **Reservation shares — industry convention**: `shareResv[k].sharesUpper` is authoritative (matches Bumtaria / Chittorgarh / IPOPremium AND the offered-shares figure exchanges' subscription APIs use). `sharesLower` is fallback (NSE PREANCHOR prints at lower band). Was preferring sharesLower until the 2026-09-22 reservation normalisation rewrote both bands from operator percentages.
  - **FOUR readers resolve this pair, and as of 2026-09-28 all four prefer UPPER.** `directOffered()` / `directOf()` (`ipoCalc.ts` and `subscription.service.ts`) always did; **`reservation()` and `bookOf()` in `apps/web/lib/api.ts` never had**, and both were fixed that day. **The convention was written down here while two readers quietly contradicted it** — so when a rule like this is recorded, grep for every reader before trusting that it holds. The two faults printed 5–7% high, and each put two different answers on the SAME page: ORIENTCABL's *Applications required for 1×* block said Retail **1,36,152** while its Live Subscription table said **1,29,144** — 7,008 apart, with the 4.93× multiple computed against the second. Verified after the fix across the whole build: **41/41 reservation legends and 38/38 forms-for-1× figures on the upper band, 0 on the lower.**
- **`shareResv[k].source` — provenance, and the reason the old guard was worthless** (2026-09-24). Values: **`exchange`** (the PREANCHOR / anchor parser, i.e. the exchange's own number) · **`operator`** (typed into the cell, usually off the RHP) · **`derived`** (Autofill or the script, from `pct × total`). **A stored count is the exchange's figure and OUTRANKS the derivation** (operator ruling) — so only `derived` rows may be refilled, and the script refuses the other two **even under `--force`**. It always had a `source === 'operator'` check, but **no row in the database had ever carried the field**, so it protected nothing and rewrote every row it looked at. `scripts/backfill-resv-source.js` stamped the rows that reproduce the derivation — **155 attributed as of 2026-09-25, 22 left** (the last 8 only became stampable once ORIENTCABL's issue-size column was fixed, since the script divides by it); the ones that don't reproduce were **LEFT ALONE on purpose** — a mismatch is not evidence of an exchange figure, and MPIMANIPAL's stored 8,844 is an Autofill snapshot of a broken ₹0.40 Cr issue size, so stamping it `exchange` would lock nonsense behind a flag that stops any future fill repairing it. The form shows the chip under each count and the drift tooltip now names which side is stale.
- **Autofill fills BLANK rows only.** Two buttons: *"Fill N blank rows"* (silent) and a separate red *"Replace all"* that appears only when counts exist and names how many came from the exchange or a human. **This is the old *"Fill shares from %"* button SPLIT, not removed** — the operator asked on 2026-09-25 why it had gone. `Replace all` **is** that button, unchanged in function; the split exists because one click used to do two very different things, and the destructive half now says so. **Re-deriving every row from the percentages is still one click.**
