- **SME autofill does NOT round to minimum-application multiples — REJECTED 2026-09-29 for want of evidence, do not re-propose without more RHPs.** The Share-Reservation spec's Phase 3 asked for it, on the strength of one SME issue whose three category counts are exact multiples of 2 / 3 / 8 lots. Two SME RHPs were obtained to test it and neither supports the change: **DPSJP** is a *draft* prospectus, fixed-price, every figure still `[●]` — no evidence at all; **DOVE SOFT** (BSE SME, 22 Sep 2026, Offer Structure p.267) states net offer 62,70,000 = QIB 64,800 + NII 30,70,800 + Individual 31,34,400, and its individual portion is `50% × net = 31,35,000` taken DOWN to 31,34,400. That is floor-to-lot and floor-to-2-lot at once (2,612 lots, an even number), so the one figure that rounds cannot tell the two rules apart. The catalogue is no help either: **every SME reservation count we hold is `derived`** — our own autofill — so measuring against it only re-reads our own rule back. Fixture kept in `minApplication.spec.ts`; the Sai Urja figures there are unaffected (they are `minApplicationShares` inputs, not a rounding claim). **What would settle it: two or three SME RHPs with a priced band, a stated lot, and a category whose raw `pct × net` is NOT already a lot multiple.**
  - Worth knowing while you are in here: SME issuers pick their own split and it is not the mainboard's. DOVE SOFT gave QIB **1.03%** of the net offer and Individual 50% — legal, because SME QIB is a ceiling ("not more than 50%") not a floor. A percentage that looks absurd beside a mainboard issue is not by itself a data fault.
- **Recovering a wrecked offer: BACK-SOLVE THE POLLER.** SPECTRAA published **₹1.00 Cr against a real ₹42.52 Cr** offer, and the record held no document, no anchor roster and no allotment rows — every figure in it traced back to the broken size, so nothing internal could falsify anything. The way out is `IpoSubscription`: the poller stores `timesSubscribed = demand ÷ the book it saw at the time`, so **`nseShares ÷ timesSubscribed` recovers the denominators the record held while it was live.** SPECTRAA gave QIB 17,08,817 · bHNI 3,43,201 · sHNI 1,71,606 against a published 17,08,800 / 3,43,200 / 1,71,600 — **under 0.01%**, which proves the record was CORRECT during bidding and was overwritten afterwards. That turns a third-party figure into a corroborated one. It works on any closed issue the poller ever saw, and it is also how ROBOKIDZ's fault was confirmed from the other side (its poller books matched the stored GROSS counts to the share, i.e. the ₹2 Cr market maker was being offered to QIB, NII and Retail as well).
  - **It was NOT the legged-issue desync, and checking that first saves an hour.** SPECTRAA's `fresh` was ₹0 and `ofs` ₹1 Cr — the legs AGREED with the broken column, so a leg-sum fix would have changed nothing. **A wrong size with consistent legs is a different fault from a wrong size with legs that disagree.**
  - **Fixing the size alone would have made it worse.** The percentages had been back-derived from the counts against the broken total and read **QIB 936.84% · bHNI 188.16% · sHNI 94.08%**; re-deriving those against a correct total gives 3.4 crore shares. Size, legs, market maker and reservation table are one repair.
  - **`scripts/fix-spectraa-offer.js`** is the record of it — sourced constants, an arithmetic reconciliation that refuses to write unless the legs sum to the total and the rows sum to the net offer, dry-run by default.
  - **`scripts/refill-derived-reservation.js`** is the reusable half: re-derives `derived` rows exactly as opening and saving the admin form would, **including carve-outs**. Use it instead of `normalise-reservation-shares.js`, which predates carve-outs (2026-09-25), divides the GROSS and is what produces this staleness. It has no `--force` — a stale derived row is a bug, a stored `exchange`/`operator` count is not.
- **Precision belongs in the data, rounding belongs at the render site** (`pctStr()` in `lib/catColor.ts`, 2026-09-29). Where an RHP states COUNTS rather than percentages, the stored percentage has to be precise enough to reproduce them **and** to satisfy B01, which tolerates only **0.001** on the sum — SPECTRAA's four categories are 49.9473 / 10.0316 / 5.0158 / 35.0053, and the published 2-dp figures (49.95 / 10.03 / 5.02 / 35.01) add to **100.01 and are REJECTED**. So the table stores 4 dp and every reservation-bar label, legend and tooltip rounds to 2 dp on the way out. Records with round percentages are unaffected — 50 still renders `50%`, not `50.00%`. **Do not "tidy" a stored percentage to make a page read better; round it where it is printed.**
- **The Offer-size summary strip reads SHARES, not lots.** `residualLots` printed raw as `1.0297029702970297 lots` (104 ÷ 101) — a stated total is not lot-aligned, so the remainder genuinely is not a whole number of lots and shares is the only honest unit.
- **`scripts/normalise-reservation-shares.js`** — dry-run by default; `--apply` writes; `--only SYM` narrows; **`--force` to touch a cell that already holds a count** (blanks only without it). Walks every IPO with a filled `pct` and recomputes `sharesUpper = pct × issueSize / priceBandMax` and `sharesLower` against priceBandMin, **both ROUNDED — not floored to lot** since 2026-09-24 (see the two-figures rule above). Refuses `exchange` and `operator` rows even under `--force`. Ran against prod 2026-09-22: 38 IPOs / 149 rows rewritten. Rows with `pct` blank are skipped (safer than guessing). **Reservation autofill button on the IPO form** — Offer & Reservation tab, above the reservation table, fills every row's sharesUpper + sharesLower from percentages + both band prices; confirms before overwriting existing overrides.
- **Reservation percentage bar (`reservation()` in ipoCalc.ts)**: reads `extra.shareResv` DIRECTLY, not through `computeIssue` — the engine's residual allocator was synthesizing a duplicate `nii` roll-up when both `hni` and `hni2` were present (NSE bar summed to 120% before the fix). Drops the synthetic nii, derives Employee pct from `sharesLower ÷ totalShares × 100` when the operator's pct is blank, routes labels through `subCatLabel()` so the bar reads "HNI (10L+)" / "HNI (2-10L)" / "Retail".
  - ~~**It preferred `sharesLower`, and ran 5–7% high on every page**~~ **FIXED 2026-09-28.** `reservation()` read `Number(row.sharesLower) > 0 ? sharesLower : sharesUpper`, **contradicting the industry-convention rule two bullets up** where `sharesUpper` is authoritative and `directOffered()` / `directOf()` both prefer it. Every recent record stores both bands, so the detail page's *Issue reservation* legend showed the FLOOR-band count on all of them — measured **+5.31% to +6.67%** (ADROITIND 59,80,556 against 56,23,507; ACEVECTOR 10,50,00,000 against 9,84,37,500). **The same page disagreed with itself**: RUNWALENTR's legend read `QIB 85.6 L` beside a Live Subscription panel that reproduced the exchange exactly. The legend was internally coherent — its ₹ figure was the floor-band value too — it simply was not the basis anyone publishes. Now upper-first, **verified on all 41 built pages that store both bands: 41 match the upper band, 0 the lower.** It also corrected the derived `pct` fallback, which had been dividing a FLOOR-band count by a CAP-band total and overstating the percentage by the width of the band. **The lesson: a convention recorded in one place and contradicted in another is worth grepping for — `directOffered` honoured it; `reservation()` and `bookOf()` never had — see the four-readers note above.
  - ~~**`reservation()` has NO concept of a carve-out**~~ **CLOSED 2026-10-09 — the last carve-out blind spot.** Reported by the operator against IPOPremium's RKFAL table: our legend summed to **94.97%** and the missing **5.02% WAS the market maker**. The cause was not a basis error — the percentages already matched the reference to the digit — but that `reservation()` iterates a fixed bucket list that **does not contain `marketmaker`**, while carve-outs live in `extra.carveouts` and not in `shareResv` at all. Carve-out rows are now appended AFTER the split rows, the order IPOPremium and Chittorgarh both print them in, and skipped when the same category already has a `shareResv` row with a count so a legacy record cannot double-count. **Verified live against the reference to the digit**: QIB 1.01% · the two HNI bands 25.08 + 12.56 = 37.64 against their combined 37.65 · Retail 56.32% · Market maker 5.02% · total 42,67,200 shares.
    - **THE COUNT NOW DECIDES THE PERCENTAGE, and that was the subtler half.** The legend printed the stored `pct` whenever one existed, and **the two are on DIFFERENT BASES**: an operator's percentage is the ICDR split, a share of the **NET** offer, while the count beside it is a share of the **GROSS**. SPECTRAA printed *QIB 49.95%* next to a count that is **47.42%** of the issue size — **a row disagreeing with itself**, on 7 records. The stored percentage survives only as the fallback where a record has percentages and no counts.
    - **Catalogue-wide: 54 of the 71 records rendering a legend now reconcile to the issue size within 0.5%**, and **23 published percentages moved** — every one from a net-basis figure to the gross-basis one that already agreed with the count shown beside it (NSE QIB 50 → 48.29 · RUNWALENTR 50 → 49.63 · SPECTRAA 49.95 → 47.42).
    - **`subCatLabel` had no `marketmaker` case** and would have rendered `MARKETMAKER`. And **`catColor` was IGNORING `--c-emp` and `--c-shareholder`** — tokens that have existed since the subscription rebuild — handing every reserved quota the generic `--brand-400`; it reads them now, plus a new **`--c-mm`** slate for the market maker, tested **BEFORE** the employee branch because **VANS carries both quotas** and they would otherwise be one swatch in the same bar.
    - 6 tests built from the stored RKFAL and SPECTRAA records.
- **THE ADMIN FORM'S *Share Reservation* TABLE IS NOW WHOLLY OFFERED-BASIS, through ONE resolver** (`shownRow` in `IpoForm.tsx`, 2026-10-09). Two operator reports on RKFAL, one screen, two unrelated causes:
  - **THE MARKET MAKER HAD NO ROW.** `RESV_ROWS` never carried the key — left out as "an SME-only reservation that was never one of the seven categories", which was true and **stopped being a reason the day the public legend started showing it**. The admin form was then the ONE surface hiding a figure the operator had entered. It is a read-only carve-out row now, like Employee / Shareholder / Policyholder; `CARVEOUT_ROWS` carries all four.
  - **THE SHARE (%) TOTAL READ 0 — because the rows and the Total read two different things.** Each row derives its percentage from the stored COUNT; the Total read `computeIssue`. **And the engine only ever sees PERCENTAGES**: `issueInputsFrom` maps `pct`, so a record stating its reservation as COUNTS hands it an empty `reservation` and gets back **no categories at all**. **24 records, EVERY ONE SME** — which is exactly how an SME RHP gets entered. **Amount Reserved and Forms-for-1X were dashed on all 24 from the same cause** and nobody had reported those.
  - **Third time this Total has been wrong and the same cause every time**: it re-derived the rows instead of summing the column it sits under. `shownCount` fixed the share column on 2026-09-30; the percentage and amount columns were left on the engine. **When you fix a total, fix every column of it.**
  - **TWO TOTAL ROWS, because there are two questions.** `Total (net offer)` sums the split rows — what the percentages add to 100% of. `Total (issue size)` adds the carve-outs back, and **its percentage is the diagnostic: it reads 100 when the table is complete** and names the shortfall when it is not. RKFAL reconciles at 42,67,200 shares / ₹34.99 Cr, exactly its stored issue size; SAIURJA read **73.42** and that is how the ex-anchor QIB fault above was found.
  - **Amount Reserved is now the shown count × the price**, not `computeIssue`'s ALLOCATION shares (floored to lot, residual absorbed). Measured before shipping: **29 records gain a figure, 4 move, 42 unchanged at 2 dp** — and three of the four move TOWARD the count printed beside them. The fourth is PRIORITY, where only the QIB row is entered so residual absorption handed QIB the whole net offer and the cell read **₹250 Cr for a 50% quota**; it now reads ₹125.16 Cr. Forms-for-1X goes through **`minApplicationShares()`**, so SAIURJA's column reads bHNI 21 / sHNI 28 / Retail 296 — its published RHP figures, cross-checking the lot ladder on the same screen.
  - E-RESV looped `CARVEOUT_ROWS` and labelled with a two-way ternary, so a **POLICYHOLDER quota would have been reported as a shareholder one**. It loops `CARVEOUT_TABLE_KEYS` — the three that actually have a legacy table row — with a label map.
- **`shareResv[].on` IS VESTIGIAL AND IS READ NOWHERE, as of 2026-10-09 — and the note that justified keeping it was FALSE.** The 2026-10-03 entry said *"every stored `on: false` row has an empty `pct`, so it changes nothing either way"*. Five mainboard records carry `on: false` on **all four split rows while holding percentages AND counts** — **ANNU · SYMBIOTEC · HYTECH · SKYWAYS · LUMINO** (SKYWAYS: QIB 49.92% / 1,49,76,000 shares) — so `reservation()` returned NO ROWS and **those five published no reservation legend at all**, `offeredByBucket()` gave them no Book Size, and `issueInputsFrom` gave the engine nothing. MOLBIO shows the stored values are simply inconsistent: `on: true`, no counts. Measured: **5 records changed, 1,266 unchanged.**
  - **The repository asserted the correct behaviour in a TEST TITLE while the assertion beneath it did the opposite** — *"a row that is explicitly off still goes, even with a percentage on it"*, expecting `toBeUndefined()`. A title is not a test. Both are now real, with SKYWAYS as the fixture.
  - **FOUR readers honoured it and the fourth is in the API** — `offeredFromIpo` in `apps/api/.../offered.ts`, which means the poller had no denominator for those five either (the W-SUB class of permanent loss; all five closed long ago, so nothing was lost). **Fixed in source and PENDING THE NEXT POOL HANDSHAKE** — it changes nothing today, so it does not justify a deploy of its own.
- **QIB Book Size is NET of the anchor BY DESIGN, so a `times` computed against the GROSS looks like a 2.5× overstatement when nothing is wrong.** I read SAIURJA's 2.80× against its gross QIB, called it "really 1.12×" and reported a fault that did not exist. The page deliberately divides by the net book — the note above has said so since 2026-09-21 (*"For QIB this yields a HIGHER Times than what NSE's own page shows"*). **This is the third variant of the same trap** after stored-vs-displayed times and offered-vs-allocation counts: **before comparing any two figures here, establish which basis each is on.**
  - **And the figures were RIGHT BY COINCIDENCE, which is the part worth keeping.** With the ex-anchor count stored, `subscriptionTable`'s `deduct < book` guard refused the deduction (586,800 > 392,400), so the page showed an already-net figure with **no** deduction applied — two errors cancelling to the correct number under a **"Gross"** label. After the repair the number is identical and the label reads *Net (post anchor) · actual*. **A correct figure is not evidence of correct data**; the guard that stopped it going negative is also what hid the fault.
  - ~~**`issueInputsFor()` NEVER POPULATES `IssueInputs.carveouts`, on web OR mobile**~~ **FIXED 2026-10-03 — `issueInputsFrom()` in `packages/shared-types/src/issueInputs.ts` is THE ONE MAPPER** and web + mobile are now one-line delegations to it. 19 tests, built from the stored RUNWALENTR and SAIURJA records; it reproduces RUNWALENTR's published net offer `1,62,78,687` and net QIB `32,55,739`, and SAIURJA's net offer `1,99,2000`, all exact. What the three-way drift had cost, measured over all 1,267 visible records:
    - **Neither copy passed `carveouts`**, so everything built on `derive(ipo)` split the **GROSS** offer — the fault fixed in the admin form on 2026-09-25 (RUNWALENTR, 57,378 shares too many into QIB) surviving in the two files nobody edited. **20 records now deduct their carve-out**, and one of the two callers — mobile's `reservation()` — is CUSTOMER-FACING.
    - **The copies had drifted, each missing what the other had**: web passed `instrument` (the W04 FPO gate) and not `policyholder`; mobile passed `policyholder` and not `instrument`. Neither passed `finalIssuePrice`, and web sent `retail` as its only discount. **A comment claiming two copies mirror each other is not a mechanism that keeps them mirrored.**
    - **`shareResv[k].on` IS VESTIGIAL, and requiring it cost more than the carve-out did.** Both mappers tested `r?.on && …` — truthy. The form has no `on` concept at all (`Resv` is `pct`/`sharesLower`/`sharesUpper`/`source`, and its engine mapping tests `pct > 0` alone), **only 13 of the 72 records with a reservation table still carry one** — legacy rows that also still hold the long-stripped `count`/`req1x`/`remark` — and **every stored `on: false` row has an empty `pct`**, so it changes nothing either way. On the records lacking the key both mappers therefore returned a reservation of **all zeros** and `derive()` produced no categories at all: **39 records go from an all-zero split to real figures.** Web's own `reservation()`, in the same file as one of those mappers, had always read `on === false` — the correct test. **Two readings of one field, in one file.**
    - **Both passed the employee / shareholder / policyholder percentages INTO the split**, where the form excludes them — so adding `carveouts` to the old mappers without removing those would have counted a quota TWICE, once off the top and once inside the split. `CARVEOUT_ROW_KEYS` and a test pin it.
    - **The rewire loses no scenario and drops no validation issue; it gains exactly ONE** — KHERIAAUTO's B18, the already-known market maker at 4.55% against the 5% floor.
    - **DATES ARE DELIBERATELY NOT PASSED, and that is a measurement**: feeding them in makes **564 of the 1,267 records raise a date issue** (559 B12, 6 B11), because T+3 is recent and most of the catalogue is historical. `admin/catalog/view` renders `issues`, so that is 559 lines of noise over four real faults. The admin FORM still passes them, which is the right place — it is editing one issue whose dates the operator is choosing.
    - **The admin form stays its OWN mapper** (three → two), because it maps live form state — strings mid-edit, a basis still being chosen — not a stored record, and it is the copy that was right all along. Keep the two in step by hand; the comment above its `inputs` object says so.
    - `parseIssueValue` was byte-identical in both apps and now delegates to **`parseIssueSizeRupees`** in the same module. A NUMBER is rupees (the `issueSize` COLUMN); `extra.issueSizeCr` is the one in crore.
  - ~~**SHIVCHEM and PAPADMALJI store a market-maker SHARE COUNT under an `amount` basis**~~ **FIXED 2026-10-03** via `scripts/fix-carveout-basis.js`. `carveouts.marketmaker` was `114000` / `144000` with `carveoutBasis.marketmaker: 'amount'`, so every reader divided **₹1,14,000 Cr against a ₹13.31 Cr issue** (8,565× the whole offer) and ₹1,44,000 Cr against ₹19.00 Cr. **It was LIVE in the admin form, not latent** — `IpoForm` has passed `carveouts` since 2026-09-25, and the engine's answer to a carve-out bigger than the offer is **no scenario at all**: `primary` undefined, every derived figure on the screen blank. It escaped notice only because neither record has reservation percentages entered. Both now derive cleanly, net offers `20,32,774` and `24,94,889`.
    - **Flipping the BASIS is the repair, not rewriting the value** — both figures are 5.31% and 5.46% of their stated share counts, which is exactly what an SME market maker is. The value was already the document's count; only the label was wrong.
    - **The generalisable test: an `amount` carve-out whose RUPEE reading exceeds the issue size cannot be rupees.** The script SCANS on that rather than naming the two symbols, because the Excel importer can reintroduce the shape. A match is still refused unless a share denominator exists and the share reading lands between 0.01% and 25% of the offer, so a different fault wearing this shape stops for a human.
    - **THE SHAPE DID COME BACK, and the scan is why it was caught in one command** (2026-10-09). **RAVITA** stores `marketmaker: "522000"` under basis `amount`, so it read as **₹5,22,000 CRORE — 4,498× the whole issue** — and once `reservation()` started rendering carve-outs it drew a **449,789%** row. 522,000 shares is **5.04%** of its offer, which is exactly what an SME market maker is: the value was always right and only the label was wrong. **A latent figure becomes an absurd one the moment something renders it — which is the argument for rendering it, not against.** (KHERIAAUTO ₹2 Cr, AXIOMGAS ₹3 Cr and BMISL ₹2 Cr remain genuine rupee figures and the scan leaves them alone.)
    - **It had been invisible to the catalogue-wide validation sweep**, because a scan that does not pass carve-outs cannot see a carve-out fault — which is why these two were not among the four the 2026-09-30 sweep reported. **Audit with the same inputs the screen uses, or the audit cannot see what the screen shows.**
- **Day-wise carries APPLICATION-wise behind a toggle** (operator ask, 2026-09-30; live 2026-10-01). `Share-wise / Application-wise` is a `.seg` control above the table — **a toggle rather than more columns**, because the table already carries six with bHNI/sHNI nested under NII, and the same component renders in the `/subscription` modal where eleven would not fit. Each application cell is the **multiple with the count received behind it** (`.dws-apps`, muted 11.5px) — both, because they answer different questions and the live Application-wise panel already shows the pair.
  - **The poller logs ONE NESTED KEY, `apps`** — `{ qib: {n, x}, … }` per entry, where `n` is applications received and `x` the by-applications multiple. Nested rather than eight more flat keys so every existing reader is untouched, and **omitted entirely when no category has bid data**, which is most of the catalogue (23 IPOs of the 318 with subscription rows carry a bid count), so the common case costs nothing. The figures were always on `SubRow`; they simply never reached the log.
  - **HISTORY CANNOT BE BACKFILLED, and it bit within a day.** Every entry logged before 2026-10-01 has no application figure and never will. **SRIT and SHAHINVEST — the two richest datasets, 20,29,212 and 2,48,323 retail applications — closed on 30 September and will never be polled again, so they will never carry it.** NITYAS's own Day 1 row renders as dashes beside a populated Day 2. Say this out loud before anyone asks why a closed issue is empty.
  - **Deploying at 18:00 wasted a day.** The poller window is 10:00–18:00 IST and the pool restarted a minute after its last tick, so nothing was written for 16 hours and the first verification attempt found an empty key. **Deploy a poller change inside its window, or expect to wait.**
  - **Verified against the table, not just by existence**: 12 of 12 categories across NITYAS and VNL matched `IpoSubscription` on all three figures (`n`, `x`, and the share-wise `×` beside them). A key appearing only proves the poller wrote something.
  - **Width, measured at both ends**: at 1280 the table fits its container exactly (634/634); at phone width it scrolls inside `.fin-scroll` — but **Share-wise already did** (357 against a 324 container), and the application view only widens it to 442. The page never scrolls sideways either way. **The Browser pane reports a ZERO-WIDTH viewport until you set one** — every width it gave before `resize_window` was meaningless.
- **Day-wise column format**: `Date | QIB | NII (bHNI + sHNI) | Retail | Total` — sub-headers under NII use the same typography as the top row (removing the `fontSize: 11, muted` inline override was needed for the values to line up under their headers), and `.fin-tab.day-wise-sub thead tr + tr th` right-aligns them (see the `:first-child` trap under Conventions). `bHNI` value reads `e.hni ?? e.nii` (fallback for legacy rows that only had NII combined); `sHNI` = `e.hni2`.
- **The Date cell names the bidding day** — `Day 1 · 22 Sept`, `Day 2 (Last) · 23 Sept` (2026-09-23). The number is the row's **position in the ascending log**, NOT `date − openDate`: a window straddling a weekend (close Tue, open Fri) would otherwise number its three bidding days 1, 4, 5. "Last" is the row whose date equals the issue's `closeDate`, which `NseIpoLanding` passes down — `DayWiseSubscription` has only the log otherwise and would have to guess.
- **`.subv2-*` CSS namespace** — shared across all three surfaces. Detail page uses `.subv2 subv2-detail` where `.subv2-detail` overrides the standalone-page paddings (`padding: 0; max-width: none; margin: 0`) so the card fits its parent column without an extra 40px bottom gap. Live Subscription-specific bits use `.lsx-*`.
- **Detail page IS slim by design**: hero KPI tiles + Key dates timeline + Issue reservation + Lot ladder + Issue details + Live Subscription + GMP + (rich content sections). No Day-wise section on the detail page — it lives on `/subscription/v2`. When the reader wants deeper history they click the `→` icon on the Live Subscription card.

## Subscription poller — cadence + tuning
- **THE SIDEBAR ENTRY IS `System Settings → Provider Keys`, NOT "Integrations"** — route `/admin/integrations`, gated on `providers.manage` (any role with `scope: all` has it). This file called it Integrations in five places and the IPO form's own hint did too, so an operator went looking for a menu that does not exist (2026-10-21). **A screen that names a location has to name what the reader will actually see**, same class as a stated limit disagreeing with the enforced one.
- **Live-tunable via `admin → System Settings → Provider Keys → 'Live subscription poller — cadence & window'`**. Provider key `subscription` with fields: `normalSec` (default 60), `fastSec` (default 5), `fastWindowMin` (default 90), `fromMin` (default 600 = 10:00), `toMin` (default 1080 = 18:00). Poller reads `providers.effective('subscription')` on each tick with the 60s config cache TTL, so overrides take effect within a minute — **no pool restart** needed for cadence tweaks. Bad values (non-numeric, ≤0) fall back to defaults per-field so a typo can't stall the loop.
- **A PER-ISSUE WINDOW OVERRIDES THE GLOBAL ONE, and the IPO FORM can set it since 2026-10-21** — `Timeline & Parties → Live subscription polling`, two `<input type="time">` boxes writing `extra.pollFromMin` / `pollToMin` as minutes from midnight. **Why it exists: SEBI's 10:00–17:00 is the rule, not a guarantee.** JPL bid outside it, and a global window either misses the first hour of such an issue or drags every other issue into hours it is not trading in. `windowFor` has read these keys since they were added; until this there was no way to set one but a database write.
  - **THE KEYS WERE IN `SERVER_OWNED_EXTRA`, WHICH WOULD HAVE MADE THE BOXES SILENTLY INERT.** That list works by re-applying the stored value OVER whatever a save sent — so shipping fields without touching it gives the operator two boxes that accept a time, report no error, and change nothing. **A control that looks like it works is worse than no control.**
  - **THE LIST IS SPLIT, NOT EMPTIED, and the split rule is MENTION rather than presence.** `SERVER_OWNED_EXTRA` keeps `subLog` · `subLogHour` · `gmpLog` · `gmpSourceId` · `qibSplit`, which no form sends and none may write. The two window keys moved to **`OPERATOR_OWNED_EXTRA`**, kept only when the payload does not NAME them. That is what removes the deploy-ordering hazard: an older client that omits them still cannot wipe a live override, and the new boxes can both set and clear — **either order of "drop the protection" and "ship the boxes" would otherwise leave a window in which one save destroys a live issue's window.**
  - **The form therefore ALWAYS sends both keys, even blank**, and **a cleared box DELETES the key** rather than storing null. A key present and falsy reads as SET to anything testing for presence — the trailing-`""` tail `mergeContentPatch` exists to avoid — and `pollMinute` would treat a stored 0 as absent anyway.
  - **EITHER END MAY BE BLANK ON ITS OWN**: that end falls back to the global and the other still applies, so *"poll until 20:00, start at the usual time"* is one box, not two.
  - **THE CLOSE IS DERIVED FROM THE WINDOW, so fast mode tracks it** — `closeMin = toMin − POLL_GRACE_MIN`. JPL's 20:00 therefore switches to 5-second polling at **17:30**, not the global 15:30. One place states a window rather than two that can disagree.
  - **`packages/shared-types/src/pollWindow.ts` owns the numbers and the acceptance test** (15 tests) — the defaults, the envelope, the `HH:MM` conversion both ways, and `pollMinute`, which `windowFor` IMPORTS rather than keeping its own copy. The form has to state limits the poller enforces, and this is the only thing that stops it explaining a rule the API does not apply. **Minutes-from-midnight never reaches the screen**: `540` is unreadable in a form box.
  - **W-POLL warns on the two ways a window silently fails.** An INVERTED pair is discarded whole and the global applies; an hour outside the sweep's own **07:00–22:00 envelope** is accepted and then never acted on, because the 10-second tick rules those hours out before it reads any issue. Both are warnings, not blockers — the window is optional and a bad one costs the default.
  - Proved through the real compiled `update()` against the real database inside a transaction that rolled back: set stored it, clear deleted the keys, an omitting client preserved them, and the five server-owned keys survived every case. **JPL carries 09:00–20:00 and is the only override in the catalogue.**
- **Window: 10:00–18:00 IST**. `OPEN_TO_MIN` is `18 * 60` — 1-hour grace past the 17:00 SEBI bidding cutoff so NSE catwise and BSE demand-schedule can serve consolidated / finalized numbers for another 30-60 min on closing day. Was 17:00 sharp until 2026-09-21 when NSE's final subscription never got written.
- **Fast mode**: last 90 min before 17:00 (i.e., from 15:30 IST) AND through the 17:00-18:00 grace hour uses `FAST_MS` (5s default) instead of `NORMAL_MS` (60s default). `BIDDING_CLOSE_MIN = 17 * 60` is the fixed regulatory anchor for the fast-window trigger; `OPEN_TO_MIN` (poller cutoff) is separate and tunable.
- **QIB HAS NO APPLICATION MULTIPLE, and the poller stored one anyway for months** (fixed 2026-10-09). `sharesPerApp` had no QIB branch and fell through to `return lotSize` — pricing an institutional bid as one retail lot — **three lines below its own docblock saying *"QIB — no per-application ratio meaningful (institutional)"*.** An Application-wise multiple is `bids ÷ applications-needed-for-1×` and needs a MINIMUM BID; QIB has none, which is why `minApplicationLots('qib')` returns 0 and why **`APP_WISE_ORDER`** (`packages/shared-types/src/appWise.ts`, 8 tests, read by both web surfaces AND mobile) leaves QIB out of the panel entirely. It returns 0 now, so nothing is stored.
  - **`scripts/clear-qib-application-multiple.js`** cleared what it had written: **22 subscription rows + 48 day-wise entries** (NITYAS · VNL · RKFAL). Backup in `C:\ProgramData\Investoyard\backups\qib-application-multiple-*.json`.
  - **THE BID COUNT IS REAL AND IS KEPT** — the exchange genuinely reported N QIB applications; only the multiple is uncomputable. `AppCellView` already renders a dash for a missing multiple beside a present count, so the day-wise cell reads `— 2`.
  - **THE FIRST COUNT WAS WRONG AND THE WAY IT WAS WRONG MATTERS.** An initial query filtered `applicationsSubscribed > 0` and reported **4** records; there were **22**, because **18 stored a literal `0`**. A stored `0` is the MORE misleading value — `0×` reads as a real measurement of no demand, while `0.12` at least looks odd enough to question. **When auditing a figure that should not exist, filter on NOT NULL, never on `> 0`.**
- **BSE per-category bids are FUDGED** (per operator's original C# workflow) — BSE ships `totalapplication` as a scalar for the whole issue. Back-solve for hni / hni2 / shareholder / **employee** from NSE avg shares-per-bid; retail absorbs the remainder. Employee was excluded from this loop until 2026-09-21 (NSE closing day) — Employee BSE bids were silently folded into Retail's residual, understating Employee applications by 12k+ and inflating Retail by the same.
- **60s config cache TTL** is the whole reason live tuning works without a restart. The `subscription` provider config's settings JSON drives cadence directly.
- **NO RESERVATION TABLE MEANS NO SUBSCRIPTION DATA, EVER — the poller's hardest prerequisite** (diagnosed 2026-10-03, when ten open SME issues had nothing). `computeSubs` opens with `if (!offeredByBucket || offeredByBucket.size === 0 || buckets.size === 0) return []`, and `offeredFromIpo()` can only build that map from `extra.shareResv`. So on a record with no percentages the exchange's demand **is fetched and thrown away on every tick** — once a minute, every 5 s after 15:30 — and nothing is stored. **The loss is permanent: a bidding day that passes without a reservation table is gone.**
  - **The proof is a clean split, not an inference**: of the 1,267 visible records, **23 carry poller-written rows (`nseShares`/`bseShares` present) and ALL 23 have a usable reservation table — zero exceptions.** The 295 that hold subscription data WITHOUT one (290 mainboard, 5 SME) have **zero** poller-written rows between them; that data came from the Excel historical import. **`subrows > 0` does not mean the poller ever worked on a record** — check for `nseShares` before concluding anything about the live path.
  - **It is NOT an SME or exchange fault, and that matters because it looks like one.** SPECTRAA, ROBOKIDZ and SAIURJA are SME and all three carry poller-written rows, so SME symbols resolve on the feed. Ten SME issues open together with nothing is the reservation-table gap wearing an SME costume.
  - **The log line that would have said so is unreachable**: `refreshIpo` warns *"no computable subscription (no demand yet, or missing reservation table / issue size / price band)"*, but `web.config` sets `loggingEnabled="false"`, so there is nowhere to read it. **This diagnosis has to come from the data.**
  - **Percentages ALONE are enough** — no share counts needed. With `pct` filled and no count, `derivedTotal` falls back to `issueSize ÷ price` and each bucket derives from its percentage. That is a cheap per-record fix, which is what makes the permanent loss worth hurrying over. **The form now warns about it — see W-SUB under IPO entry.**
  - **ALL TEN CLOSED EMPTY.** DSL · EIMS · EVENTIONS · OMARA · SHREETNB · SJP · SOLLFEGE closed 5 Oct and ACMERAIL · PARASYNTEX · TNA closed 6 Oct, every one with no reservation percentages, **0 subscription rows and 0 poller rows**. Ten issues' history, gone and unrecoverable. That is what W-SUB and the two fixes below exist to prevent recurring.
  - **THE DAILY CHECK — `GET /admin/subscription/readiness`, rendered as a card at the TOP of the admin dashboard** (2026-10-06). W-SUB only helps someone who opens the IPO form; these ten were lost silently over nine days because nobody did, so the warning has to come to the operator instead. The card lists every not-yet-closed issue the poller cannot compute, with **trading days already lost and trading days left** — which is what separates an emergency from a note — the board badge, the actual blocker named, and an *Enter it* link straight to the edit screen.
    - **It calls `offeredFromIpo()` itself rather than re-testing the conditions**, and that is the whole design. An alert that decides for itself what "cannot be computed" means drifts from the thing it is warning about, which this codebase has paid for four times over (three mappers into `computeIssue`, four readers of `sharesUpper`). If the poller would write nothing the row is listed; if the poller would work it is not. **They cannot disagree.**
    - **It renders NOTHING when there is nothing to say.** A card that is permanently present and permanently empty is a card people stop reading — which is how the thing it warns about happened.
    - **`ipos.view`, not `rails.manage`**: the people who fix this are the ones who enter IPO data, and Staff carries the IPO permissions rather than the rail ones. An alert only the superadmin can see is an alert nobody acts on.
    - **`tradingDaysBetween()` is in `packages/shared-types/src/holidays.ts`** with 7 tests, inclusive of both ends. PARASYNTEX ran 30 Sep → 6 Oct, which is **four** bidding days (30 Sep · 1 · 5 · 6 Oct — the 2nd was Gandhi Jayanti, the 3rd and 4th a weekend), not seven calendar days and not five weekdays. Outside `HOLIDAY_YEARS` it degrades to weekends-only rather than becoming a calendar count, which a test pins — the list is regenerated every January and only ever holds the current year.
    - Verified against live data on the day: it listed PARASYNTEX · ACMERAIL · TNA (lost 4, 1 left) and correctly stayed silent on **RKFAL**, which has share counts and no percentages and which the poller handles fine.
  - ~~**TWO CODE GAPS in `offeredFromIpo()`**~~ **BOTH FIXED 2026-10-06**, and `offeredFromIpo()` is now **`apps/api/src/modules/subscription/offered.ts`** with **15 tests** — it had none, and it had produced three operator reports. **Measured against the real compiled function across all 1,268 visible records: ZERO change**, so nothing live moved; both fixes only reach records that cannot work today.
    - **A FIXED-PRICE record had no price at all.** It read `priceBandMax` only, and on a fixed-price issue the one price sits in `priceBandMin` with the cap **null** — so **SJP and SOLLFEGE were blocked TWICE and entering percentages would not have helped them.** Proved with a counterfactual: given a 50/10/5/35 split, both return **NULL under the old code** and real denominators under the new one (SJP QIB 16,61,940 · SOLLFEGE 18,80,000), while book-built SHREETNB is identical either way. The same `cap || floor` fallback now also feeds the ₹ carve-out divisor and `computeSubs`'s `shniMinShares`, which had the identical gap.
    - **The stated share count is honoured — but ONLY when `totalSharesSource === 'operator'`, and that caveat is the whole finding.** The plan was to follow the documented resolver (*stated count first*) verbatim. Measuring it first caught **NITYAS**, a LIVE record whose denominator would have moved 0.065%: its `totalShares` is stamped **`derived`**, our own `round(₹ total ÷ cap)` auto-fill, and it had gone **stale** — 1,44,65,333 implies ₹108.49 Cr against a stored ₹108.42 Cr. **A stale derivation of the issue size is strictly worse than the current issue size**, so provenance decides it, exactly as `shareResv[].source` has since 2026-09-24. **7 records carry `operator`, 34 carry `derived`** — so this is a guard for figures read off an RHP, not a repair of anything today.
      - **The lesson is about the rule, not the record**: "stated `extra.totalShares` first" is written down in three places and is only safe because the field *also* records who stated it. **A precedence rule over a self-filling field is incomplete without its provenance test** — and the way to find that out is to measure the change before shipping it, not to implement the sentence.
    - The service cannot be imported into a spec at all — it pulls in Prisma's `Decimal`, which does not compile under the test tsconfig — which is the same reason `parseAnchorRows` was split out of `parseAnchor` and `offerTotals()` left `IpoForm.tsx`. **A function nothing can reach is a function nobody writes a test for.**
- **An EMPTY read is missing data, not zero demand** (`keepKnownFigures`, 2026-09-23). The write is DESTRUCTIVE — `deleteMany` + `createMany` — so whatever `computeSubs` returns becomes the entire truth for the issue, and `computeSubs` emits `timesSubscribed: 0` with no share columns for a category the exchange said nothing about (it only sets `nseShares` when `r.ns > 0`). One empty tick therefore erased a real figure permanently: ELEVATE's QIB held 32,25,142 shares — the number every other site showed and the number our own built page still carried — and was replaced by 0 for the rest of the day. **That is what "the count shows then goes to zero on refresh" means**: the page renders the baked figure, then the 60s refresh pulls the zeroed row. ADROITIND lost `1,06,116 → 1,03,008` the same way. Subscription inside the window is CUMULATIVE, so a category cannot go from 3.2 million shares to nothing. The guard carries a category forward when a tick reads **completely** empty (no shares either side AND times 0) while a real figure is stored, and re-adds a category dropped from the response entirely; both log a warning. **A SMALLER number still overwrites a bigger one** — exchanges do revise down, and clamping to the day's maximum would freeze a correction we could only undo by hand.
- **The poller writes `extra` with a jsonb MERGE, and must never go back to a read-modify-write** (2026-09-25). `refreshIpo` used to write `extra: { ...ex, subLog, subLogHour }`, where `ex` was read at the TOP of the method — **two network round trips earlier**, to NSE and to BSE. Everything written to `extra` inside that gap was silently reverted by the poller's stale copy, on every LIVE issue, once a minute — and **once every FIVE SECONDS after 15:30**, when fast mode starts. It is now `SET extra = COALESCE(extra,'{}'::jsonb) || $1::jsonb`, evaluated inside Postgres against the CURRENT row, so the window does not exist and the poller cannot touch a key it did not write.
  - **This is `SERVER_OWNED_EXTRA` enforced from the other side**: that whitelist stops the form deleting the poller's four keys; this stops the poller deleting everything else. Both faults are the same shape — two writers, one unschema'd JSON column, last write wins the whole thing.
  - **The operator's own saves were at risk, which is the part that matters.** `ipo.service.update()` is also a read-modify-write of this column, so a Book Size corrected mid-issue could vanish with nothing to show it ever landed — on a live IPO, which is exactly when an operator edits one.
  - **How it surfaced, and the lesson**: a provenance backfill reported *8 rows stamped across 2 IPOs* and only ORIENTCABL's four survived; ACEVECTOR's were gone before the next read. **A script that reports success has only proved it issued the write** — re-read after writing to a live record, or the loss is invisible. The merge was proved on a closed record before deploy: 57 keys in, 59 out, **none lost**, `shareResv` untouched.
  - **The rule applies to YOUR scripts too, not just the poller.** Any one-off that writes `extra` on an OPEN issue must merge — `UPDATE "Ipo" SET extra = COALESCE(extra,'{}'::jsonb) || $1::jsonb WHERE id = $2` — because the poller is rewriting that column every 60s (every 5s after 15:30) and a Prisma `update({ data: { extra } })` sends the whole object. Setting RUNWALENTR's `employeeDiscount` on 2026-09-28 used the merge and its 16 hourly buckets were still there afterwards; a read-modify-write would have taken whatever the poller wrote in between.
  - `Ipo` carries **no RLS policy** (`setup-rls.sql` scopes User · InvestorProfile · Consent · Application · WatchlistItem · DeviceToken), so a raw statement on that table needs no tenant GUC. A raw write to any of those six does — see the `runUnscoped` note under Conventions.
  - **ONLY `extra` moved to raw SQL. `subscriptionAsOf` stays on the Prisma path in BOTH branches**, and that is not fussiness: the column is `timestamp without time zone` and the two writers disagree about what it means. Prisma stores **IST wall-clock** (`14:08`), a raw parameter stores **true UTC** (`08:37`). Writing it in the raw statement split the column between the two branches — caught in verification on 2026-09-25 with ACEVECTOR at `08:37:28` beside ORIENTCABL at `14:08:20`, seconds apart in real time. **Moving one column to raw SQL must not silently redefine another.** (The rows self-healed within a minute, because the next poll rewrites the timestamp either way.)
  - **Proved in production, not just in theory**: a sentinel key merged onto ACEVECTOR's `extra` while it was live survived the next poller write 50s later, 60 keys → 61, `shareResv.qib.source` still `derived`. Before the fix that key is exactly what vanished.
- ~~**`subscriptionAsOf` reads 5½ HOURS FAST**~~ **FIXED 2026-09-25 — and it was the whole database, not one column.** The operator reported *"Updated 25-Sep-2026 8:19 PM"* at 2:49 PM. The chain: the column was **naive** (`timestamp without time zone`), the connection runs at `Asia/Calcutta` so the driver's `…T14:51+05:30` lost its offset and Postgres stored the **IST wall-clock**; Prisma read that back **as if UTC**; `toDetail()` stamped it `Z` with `.toISOString()`; and `fmtDateTime()` rendered it with `getHours()` — the viewer's zone. **Stamped in IST, labelled UTC, rendered back into IST**, so +5:30 landed exactly once.
  - **All 76 timestamp columns were naive** and **eight** UI sites render one in local time (the subscription card plus allotment import, audit-live, GMP log, message-template log, partner applications). Fixing only the reported field would have left a mixed database — the same trap as the poller's two branches.
  - **`ALTER … TYPE timestamptz USING col AT TIME ZONE 'Asia/Kolkata'`** reads each stored value *as IST*, which is what it actually is, so **the type change corrects every row as it converts** — no separate backfill and no half-converted window. `scripts/timestamps-to-timestamptz.js` (dry-run by default) does all of them in ONE transaction.
  - **No code deploy was needed.** The running app, whose Prisma client was generated against `timestamp`, wrote correct instants into the converted column immediately — verified against the live poller before touching the other 75. The `@db.Timestamptz(3)` annotations exist only so a future `prisma db push` cannot revert the columns.
  - **THE TRAP, and it was live for minutes: four DATE-ONLY columns.** `Ipo.openDate` · `closeDate` · `allotmentDate` · `listingDate` hold midnight. Converted, `2026-09-25 00:00` became `00:00+05:30` = **18:30Z on the 24th**, and `day()` (`toISOString().slice(0,10)`) read **one day early** — which on a close date flips a LIVE issue to closed. Fixed by pinning them to UTC midnight of their IST date (`((col AT TIME ZONE 'Asia/Kolkata')::date)::timestamp AT TIME ZONE 'UTC'`, written that way so a re-run is a no-op). **A date living in a timestamp column does not survive a timezone conversion — find those columns FIRST** (every non-null value at IST midnight is the test) and treat them separately.
  - **Precision is part of the type.** A plain `TYPE timestamptz` produced precision **6** while Prisma wants **(3)**, so `prisma migrate diff` still showed 76 alterations. Re-pinned at `timestamptz(3)`; the diff is now *"This is an empty migration"*, which is the only proof that schema and database agree.
  - **Verified against a snapshot that predates the change**: 60 of 60 IPOs' open dates match the static export built before the conversion, and the card now reads 3:02 PM at 3:03 PM.
- **Manual refresh returning `0 of 3 open updated` is NORMAL**: `refreshIpo` returns `true` only when numbers CHANGE, `false` when they're identical to the last snapshot (writes-on-change gate). "Updated" ≠ "polled" — all 3 IPOs are still being queried.

## Claude AI integration — one API key, three surfaces
- **`@anthropic-ai/sdk`** installed in `apps/api`. All AI features read the API key from `providers.effective('ai')` — one Anthropic key stored per tenant in **admin → System Settings → Provider Keys → Claude AI**, encrypted at rest, vaulted. Default model for cost/speed workloads is `claude-haiku-4-5`; the WhatsApp bot uses the configured default.
- **Three surfaces read the same key**:
  1. `IpoNoteRewriteService` — rewrites merchant-banker prose (description / strength / objects) into Investoyard's voice via `/admin/ipo-import/rewrite-note-field`. Field-by-field so a hallucination in one doesn't pollute others.
  2. `AnchorVisionService` — fallback for the Anchor Investor Intimation Letter when text extraction returns 0 items (scanned PDFs — NSE files it as an imaged signed copy). Anthropic Messages API accepts PDF bytes as a `document` block; one call, whole PDF, back comes JSON in the same `ParsedAnchor` shape the review modal already handles. `~₹3.5 per letter` at Haiku 4.5. Controller flow: text-based parser first, then Vision **whenever the ROSTER is empty** — free path stays fast on born-digital letters. **File size limit is 15 MB** for this endpoint (scanned rosters run bigger than the original 5 MB).
  - **The gate used to require no header AND no rows, and that let a readable letter fail silently** (fixed 2026-09-28). SHAHINVEST's letter is born-digital and extracts perfectly — 326 text items — but three parser patterns had drifted from it, so the price parsed (₹167) while every roster row failed. `noHeader` was therefore false, Vision never ran, and the operator got an empty roster with nothing to explain it. **The roster is the point of the upload**: if it is empty the text parser has not done its job, whatever else it picked up. **"The PDF cannot be read" is almost never the extractor** — check `extractPdfText` output before reaching for Vision.
- **The anchor parser now has 30 tests, and it needed them** (`nse-parsers/anchor.spec.ts`, 2026-09-28). Three patterns had drifted from the documents they parse and nothing caught it until an operator uploaded a letter that produced an empty roster:
  - **`TAIL` demanded a `%` sign and two decimals.** SHAHINVEST writes `4,49,650 27.76 167 7,50,91,550`, the earlier letters `53,21,739 12.50% 429.00 44,00,01,276.00`. Both now parse; each group must still START with a digit, because `[\d,]+` matches a bare comma and the TOTAL row extracts as `16 , 19,760 … 27 , 04 , 99,920`.
  - **`HEADER_TOTAL` demanded the number immediately after "allocation of"**; the letter says "allocation of **an aggregate of** 16,19,760 Equity Shares". Fillers are ENUMERATED (`an|the|up to|total|aggregate|of`, ≤4) — a wildcard would span into the next sentence and pick up the wrong figure.
  - **`stripSrNo` needed TWO spaces after the number**, so `3. COMPACT STRUCTURE FUND` kept its serial. Now punctuation (`1.` `10)`) needs no gap while an unpunctuated `2   …` still does — which keeps `3M CORP LIMITED` intact.
  - **A wrapped name may contain a DIGIT** — `ANUBHUTI VALUE FUND 2` is a real fund, and rejecting any continuation row with a digit threw away half that investor's name. The test is now for a row that looks like a FIGURE (4+ digits/commas, or a decimal).
  - **`parseAnchorRows(rows)` is split out of `parseAnchor(pdf)`** so the fixture is the letter's real extracted text — padding, wrapped names and all. **A parser that only accepts a PDF is a parser nobody writes a test for**, which is how all four faults survived.
  - **Known remaining limit**: when a name wraps BOTH sides of its numeric row *and* part of it sits on that row, only the on-row part is taken (SUNRISE reads `SUNRISE INVESTMENT`, not the full trust name). Joining unconditionally would corrupt its neighbour, whose own continuation row sits between them — genuinely ambiguous without x-positions, and the review modal is where the operator fixes it.
  3. `WhatsappAiService` — Level 2 chatbot answers.
  4. `CardVisionService` (`ipo-import/card-vision.service.ts`, 2026-10-01) — reads a one-page **IPO summary card IMAGE** into Add-IPO form fields. **Vision is the ONLY route here, not a fallback**: the cards are JPEG/PNG with no text layer at all, unlike the anchor letter where a text parser runs first. Entry point is the *Fill from IPO card* button on the IPO form; `CardReviewModal` shows every parsed value beside what the form already holds, rows that would OVERWRITE an existing value flagged in red. Route is **`POST /admin/ipo-import/parse/card`**. **14 tests** on the JSON defence (`card-vision.spec.ts`, via the exported `parseCardJsonForTest`).
     - **THE FIRST REAL CARD FOUND FOUR FAULTS IN ONE UPLOAD** (HD Fire Protect, 2026-10-07). It had been deployed since 1 Oct and never run on an image, and it had no tests; every one of the four would have been caught by one. **A feature that compiles, boots and answers its route has been shown to do none of its job.**
       - **False OVERWRITE badges on six of nine rows.** The modal compared RENDERED STRINGS: `50%` vs `50`, `2026-10-13` vs `2026-10-13T10:00`, `71,138 shares` vs `71138`, `A, B, C` vs `String(array)` with no spaces. Rows compare on the VALUE now — numbers stripped of currency and separators, dates on their day, lists sorted and case-folded — and a row that AGREES says so with a green *matches* chip. **A badge that cries wolf on two rows in three trains the operator to tick past the one that matters**, and on this card that was Big HNI.
       - **Big HNI read as the COMBINED HNI line** — 15% against a true 10%, which would have published wrong figures. Cards print `HNI 15%` above its two sub-lines. The prompt now spells the sub-line rule out twice AND `flagCombinedHni()` catches it arithmetically: the four categories coming to ~105 while dropping Small HNI gives exactly 100 is unambiguous. It **DROPS** the suspect figure rather than correcting it to `hni − hni2` — this parser never computes a value the card does not print, a dropped field leaves the form alone, and a corrected one would arrive pre-ticked and be believed.
       - **The OFS leg reported as FRESH.** The card reads *"-----No Fresh Issue-----"* above *"26,284,500 OFS Shares worth ₹712.13 Cr."* and the model put those shares in `fresh`. The prompt now states that Issue Detail has ONE LINE PER LEG, that a figure attaches only to the leg named on its own line, and that *"No Fresh Issue"* means omit the leg rather than fill it from the other one.
       - **The three bidding dates vanished on apply**, which is what "not being fetched" meant — they parsed fine. `anchorDate`/`openDate`/`closeDate` are **`datetime-local`**, and writing a bare `2026-10-13` into one makes it INVALID so the control renders empty. Apply keeps the existing time (it is the operator's; a card never states hours) and falls back to the SEBI window — 10:00 anchor and open, 17:00 close.
     - **A LEG CARRIES ITS UNIT ON BOTH SIDES, and an EQUIVALENT leg is not a change worth proposing.** The form holds one value with a Cr/Shares dropdown; the card may state the same leg the other way. The row used to take its heading from the CARD's basis and print a bare `712.13` beside it, which reads as the form holding 712.13 SHARES — the modal was never passed the form's bases at all. Each side now states its own unit, and the row works out at the band price whether the two are **the same offer**: `2,62,84,500 × ₹271 = ₹712.31 Cr` against a stated `₹712.13 Cr`, 0.025% apart, inside a 1% tolerance the band and the card's own rounding need. An equivalent leg reads **matches** and arrives **UNTICKED** — visible, explained, not applied. **Proposing a rewrite of a correct field is the same failure as a false overwrite badge: it teaches the operator to stop reading the ticks.**
       - Worth knowing: **this card is internally inconsistent** — it states ₹712.13 Cr while its own share count implies ₹712.31. Two digits transposed, on the card, not in our reading.
     - **The card's "Application Reserved" row carries the ~4.8× error this codebase fixed on 2026-09-29** — it divides Big-HNI shares by the SMALL-HNI minimum bid. Measured on two real cards: VNL bHNI 1,643 against a true 344, NITYAS 513 against 108. Retail matched to the application on both; sHNI differed by one on NITYAS because the card FLOORS where applications-to-reach-1× CEILS. So it comes back under **`crossCheck`**, rendered for comparison and **never appliable** — `req1x` is not a storable field anyway (the form strips it on save AND on load). **A disagreement there is expected, not a fault.**
     - **The card prints Small HNI BEFORE Big HNI and we store the opposite**, so the prompt names the mapping explicitly (`hni` = Big, `hni2` = Small). That is the first thing to check if a read looks wrong.
     - **`CARD_MIMES` / `CardMime` / `isCardMime` are exported so the controller's whitelist and the SDK call cannot drift** — the SDK types `media_type` as exactly that union, which is what caught a plain `string` here.
     - Every field is optional and a malformed one is DROPPED rather than written through: a card that omits a figure must leave the form's value untouched, never blank it.
- **If the key isn't configured**: the fallback surfaces a friendly `Add an API key in admin → System Settings → Provider Keys → Claude AI` error — same message the rewrite feature uses, so the operator knows where to fix it.

## ASBA print — flatten guarantee for WhatsApp forwarding
- **`fillAsbaAcroForm()` in `apps/api/.../asba-acroform.ts`** guarantees a **fully-flat PDF** on the `flatten=true` path. When per-field appearance streams don't all build (ARDEE-style blanks missing `/DR`/`/DA`), fast `form.flatten()` bails — the code then **burn-ins** each field's value at its widget `/Rect` using the embedded Helvetica AND removes every widget annotation from every page's `/Annots` array (both live widgets AND dangling `PDFRef`s from the earlier bad-widget cleanup). Then deletes the AcroForm shell.
- **Why widgets matter**: `pdf-lib`'s `form.removeField()` only unlinks a widget's annotation from a page when the widget's `/P` (page ref) is set. Many SEBI templates skip `/P`, so removeField leaves orphan widgets. Viewers still render highlighted empty form-field boxes over the burned-in text — the PDF LOOKS half-filled. The belt-and-braces sweep of every page's `/Annots` catches both live widget dicts AND dangling refs. ELEVATE_TEST.pdf: 38 orphan widgets → 12 (dangling) → **0** ✓.
- **Why this exists**: WhatsApp's in-app viewer (Android + iOS) **ignores `/NeedAppearances`**. Adobe Reader / Chrome / iOS Files honour it and regenerate appearances at open time; WhatsApp doesn't. Every partner-API PDF is forwarded through WhatsApp by the operator's brokers, so `/NeedAppearances` fallback = customer receives a blank form. Guaranteed flat = works everywhere.

## Industry master + Staff role
- **`IndustryMaster`** Prisma model — mirrors `SectorMaster` (name unique, optional `sector` roll-up, description, rank, active). Admin sidebar entry at `/admin/masters/industries`, permission `masters.industries.manage`. IPO form's Industry field is now a `SearchSelect` with inline "+ Add new" (was a plain text input). Backfilled from every IPO's `extra.industry` value via `scripts/backfill-industries.js` — 172 distinct industries created from 1,237 IPOs.
- **Staff role perms** (via `ensure-roles.ts`): granted `ipos.import` (for PREANCHOR / Anchor Intimation / IPO Note parsers), `masters.sectors.manage`, `masters.industries.manage`, `masters.lead-managers.manage`, `masters.anchors.manage`, `masters.registrars.manage`, `masters.ipo-category.manage`. Inline "+ Add new" on every picker on the IPO form now works for Staff.
- **`/admin/upload/doc`** accepts EITHER `tenants.manage` OR `ipos.manage` via `@RequireAnyPermission`. Was `tenants.manage` alone — Staff got 403 on every DRHP / RHP / prospectus file upload, and the URL never landed in form state, so Save wrote 0 documents and completeness stayed at "missing" indefinitely. Two operator reports lead-in to this one fix.

## Detail page completeness + `/ipos` list handler
- **Two ways a check leaves the score, and they are NOT the same** (`ipo-completeness.ts`, 2026-09-23): **`excluded`** = shown in the breakdown with its own count, never scored · **`skipped`** = neither shown nor counted, for a check that doesn't apply to this issue at all. `active` filters on both; `checks` still carries the excluded rows so the modal can render them.
- **A THIRD CHECK STATE: `na` — SHOWN, but counting NOWHERE** (2026-10-07). `legFilled()` requires a POSITIVE value, so a Fresh/OFS leg the operator had declared absent could never be satisfied and the score sat under 100% with nothing they could do about it. **The distinction from `excluded` is the whole point**: an excluded check is REAL WORK that simply does not belong in a page-completeness score — an ASBA template can be uploaded, so a ✗ against it is honest — while an `na` check can never be satisfied by anyone, and a ✗ there says *"missing"* about something that does not exist. `na` leaves the score AND its group's `n/m`, renders a dash plus a *not applicable* chip, and keeps the row visible so *"this issue has no fresh leg"* is something the operator can SEE.
  - **THE DECLARATION IS A UNIT PLUS A ZERO** — Cr or Shares on the dropdown with a value of exactly `0`. **It is NEVER inferred from the other leg being filled**, and that was the first attempt: *"OFS entered, Fresh not"* is the identical stored state to *"OFS entered, Fresh not reached yet"*, so inferring would hide a real gap behind a *"nothing to do"*. **A wrong ✗ is noise; a wrong n/a is an item nobody is ever prompted about again** — which makes the false negative the more expensive error, and the one to design against.
  - **"Not set" CANNOT carry the meaning, and persisting it was my wrong counter-proposal.** `basis: 'none'` is the field's DEFAULT, so saving it would stamp every untouched record with the declaration and recreate the ambiguity it was meant to remove. The operator proposed the unit-plus-zero instead: a **positive act** nobody performs by accident, needing no change to what gets saved (`str(0)` is `'0'`, so it round-trips today). **When a flag has to mean "the operator decided this", check whether the value you are about to trust is also the value they get for doing nothing.**
  - **0 records render `na` today**, because nobody has entered a zero yet — which is correct and is the point. The 263 single-leg records (247 fresh-only, 16 OFS-only) stay pending until someone declares the missing leg, and the 902 with neither leg are untouched. The inference version had marked all 263 complete on a guess.
- **The three Application rail checks are `excluded`** — ASBA PDF template · print form series · bid rail integration. They gate whether an issue can be APPLIED for, not whether its page is complete, and no report reads them, so a fully documented issue sat at 88% because nobody had uploaded an ASBA template. Score is **22 checks over 6 scored groups**; the rail still renders as `Application rail 2/3` with a **"not counted"** chip on a dashed, dimmed card, and its count never turns green because there is nothing there to complete. **Removing them outright was the first attempt and was wrong** — the operator wants the rail state at a glance, just not in the bar; that is precisely the distinction `excluded` exists for.
- **Completeness DRHP / RHP check** (in `apps/web/lib/ipo-completeness.ts`) counts a row as filled when EITHER the row's `type` matches (case-insensitively — form saves 'DRHP' / 'RHP' uppercase per `DOC_TYPES`) OR the URL text contains `drhp` / `rhp[._-]/` / `prospectus`. Handles the mislabeled-row case (operator picks "Other" but pastes a SEBI DRHP URL — should still count).
- **`/ipos` list handler now includes `documents`** — was silently omitting the join, so the admin catalog's completeness bar saw `documents: []` regardless of what was in the `IpoDocument` table. 495 of 1,237 IPOs had docs saved but were showing missing until this was fixed. `detail()` had always included them; `list()` didn't.

## Allotment list — TM name, export, and the header capture (2026-10-13)
- **`AllotmentRecord.tmName` — the trading member, which was never captured.** The parser mapped twelve logical fields and TM was not one, so it was not in the database and no amount of UI work could show it. Added end to end: schema column, header candidates, list column, export column. **Null on all 2.26 lakh existing rows — only a RE-IMPORT fills them.**
  - **THE CANDIDATES ARE PROVISIONAL AND SAY SO IN THE SOURCE.** The only registrar file on this box when they were written was a truncated upload that would not parse, so none was checked against a real document. An unmatched column leaves the field null; it never writes a wrong value.
- **`AllotmentImport.headers` — the file's OWN header row, stored verbatim, and this is the part that matters.** Registrars ship far more columns than we map and name them differently from one another, so the honest way to add a field is to read what the document says rather than guess. **One real upload now names every column, permanently, for every registrar.** The worker reports it on stdout as `H <json>` beside the existing `P` and `DONE` lines; a malformed one is swallowed, because a diagnostic must never cost an import.
- **Export — `GET /admin/allotment/records.csv`, STREAMED and cursor-paged in 5,000-row batches.** Verified on VNL: **2,26,552 rows, 18.98 MB, several batches**. A single `findMany` — or a real `.xlsx`, which must be held whole in memory — is the same mistake that already forces the PARSER into its own process. CSV with a **UTF-8 BOM**, because without it Excel reads the system codepage and mangles non-ASCII names.
  - **ONE filter builder, `recordWhere()`, shared by the list and the export**, so a download is the rows on screen rather than a second opinion about what the filters meant. Proved rather than asserted: a `status=allotted` export returned **84,694** against a list total of **84,694**.
  - **Escaping checked across the whole table, not a sample**: all **2,26,553** lines carry exactly 12 columns under quote-aware counting, so no registrar name with a comma shifts its row.
  - The button is an **`<a href>`, never a fetch** — a 1M-row IPO runs to hundreds of MB and must not sit in a browser variable.
- **`tmName` is APPENDED at index 11 of the worker's row tuple, never inserted.** An archive written before this date has 11-element rows, so `r[11]` is undefined and `|| null` makes it a null column rather than a crash. Restore was checked against that shape.
- **Upload limit 105 MB → 126 MB, and the worker heap 4 GB → 6 GB WITH IT** (operator ask, 2026-10-13: "allow up to 120 MB"; 126 is 120 plus headroom so a file the operator calls 120 MB is never refused on a rounding difference).
  - **RAISING THE LIMIT ALONE WOULD HAVE MADE FAILURES WORSE.** A 1M-row XLSB already needs ~3 GB, so a file half again as large would have cleared the upload check and then died INSIDE the parse worker — trading a clear "too big" at the door for an opaque failure after a multi-minute upload. The box has 24 GB with ~14 GB free and the worker is short-lived.
  - **IIS was never the constraint and is unchanged at 200 MB.** Proved rather than assumed: a **115 MB POST reached the Nest guard (401, not IIS's 404.13) in 1.9 s**, before and after the change.
  - **CLOUDFLARE CAPS REQUEST BODIES AT 100 MB BELOW ENTERPRISE, so 120 MB may never arrive** — every site on this box is Cloudflare-fronted. The evidence is local, not theoretical: a **70.4 MB `.xlsb` landed in `allotment-tmp` on 2026-10-13 with NO `AllotmentImport` row and a truncated zip** (`Bad compressed size: 0 != 431`), i.e. the transfer was cut off **well under the old 105 MB limit**. Going past 100 MB needs an Enterprise plan, a grey-clouded upload host, or chunked upload — **it is not a number in our source**, and the comment on `MAX_FILE` says so.
  - The limit is stated in FOUR places and all four moved: `MAX_FILE`, the `web.config` comment, the import page's docblock and its visible `sub` line, plus the API client's JSDoc. **A stated limit that disagrees with the enforced one is how an operator learns to distrust the screen.**
- **Still open**: *"show all the columns I upload"*. Deliberately NOT guessed — it is the decision `AllotmentImport.headers` exists to inform, since what the columns ARE is exactly what the choice (named fields vs a JSON blob per row) hinges on. Ask after one real upload.

## Domain & compliance rules (MUST follow)
- **Self-PAN rule:** every RAIL application uses that person's own PAN + demat + bank/UPI; one public (retail/HNI) application per PAN per IPO **plus** one per reserved quota (shareholder/employee). Print-PDF paths are validation-free by operator policy.
- **GMP** = unofficial/unregulated → always with the disclaimer; OFF for gmp-disabled tenants; no kostak/sauda quotes.
- **Distribute + inform, don't advise** — no recommendations, ratings or review scores (no RIA registration).
- **DPDP** — just-in-time collection, explicit consent (single-checkbox itemized wording is in use — flag for counsel), PII vault-tokenized.
- **Partner-commission module DEFERRED** until the SEBI referral/AP regime settles.

## Application rail
- NSE adapter: verified WEB API v1.20.5. **BSE iBBS is NOT a blind template** (checked 2026-09-02): the document arrived and the adapter matches it field for field — action codes `N`/`M`/`D`, the endpoints we call (`/login`, `/ipoorder`, `/ipoorderbulk`), and the payload keys (`actioncode`, `applicantname`, `applicationno`, `asba_upiid`, `cuttoffflag`, `clientbenfid`, `dpid`, `quantity`, `rate`, `scripid`). It was written against this same document family, not guessed. What it lacks is a UAT run, plus endpoints the doc offers that we never implemented: **`ipoupistatus`** (the UPI-status pull the Refresh button needs), `ipoappenquiry` / `ipodpupienquiry` for reconciliation, `ipodeltadownload`, and the **IPO Bid Upload API** (v1.05.4) that would give us the file channel. UAT and production base URLs are both stated in it. ALL exchange docs — the NSE zip and both BSE PDFs — live at `D:\Claude\IPO Application\`, deliberately OUTSIDE the repo: they are vendor-confidential and the BSE pair is footer-marked BSE-INTERNAL. Member creds live in admin Exchange Rails (subscription polling always uses the operator's own member; bidding routes by the IPO's active online-series member).

### UPI payment status — the live status an investor sees (2026-10-08)
- **`Application.upiStatusText` + `upiStatusAt` existed since the initial commit and NEITHER ever worked.** The column documented as *"the exchange's own UPI wording, kept verbatim"* was written `String(record.upiPaymentStatus)` — the numeric CODE, so it held `"100"` — and only by the operator's manual Refresh. `upiStatusAt` was written by **nothing at all**, and the admin Bidding Report has been rendering a permanently-empty as-of value. **The authoritative PUSH path (the exchange calling us) wrote neither.** A field existing in the schema and typed into a report column is not evidence that anything fills it — **grep for the WRITER before exposing a column to customers**, or `"100"` is what the investor reads.
- **`UPI_STATUS_LABEL` / `upiStatusLabel()` in `rail-callback.dto.ts` is the ONE code→sentence map**, verbatim from **NSE WEB API v1.20.5**, the `upiPaymentStatusFlag` table on `/v1/apppaystatus` (the spec is on this box at `D:\Claude\IPO Application\_nse_extracted\eipo_web.txt` — **read it rather than inventing wording**; the example quoted in this file for years, *"Request sent to Investor UPI App for Acceptance"*, is NOT what the spec says, which is just *"Request Sent"*).
  - **It exists because `mapUpiStatus` is deliberately lossy**: eleven codes collapse onto five of our statuses, and **the three rejections an investor can act on differently all land on `rejected`** — *Rejected by Investor* (they declined it), *Rejected due to invalid UPI* (a fixable typo), *Rejected by Sponsor Bank*. So the sentence is stored BESIDE the status, never derived from it, and the UI renders both.
  - **An unknown code returns `null`**, never a stringified number and never a guessed phrase: showing an investor `"47"` reads as a fault in the app and a guess could be wrong about their money. `mapUpiStatus` already keeps an unknown code non-terminal, so the bid is unaffected and the UI falls back to our own status line.
  - NSE's vocabulary. **BSE's per-bid pull (`ipoupistatus`) is still unimplemented — confirm its codes against this table rather than assuming they match**; only the NSE adapter populates `upiPaymentStatus` today.
- **Both paths now do ONE identical write** through `ApplicationRepo.updateStatus` (patch type `StatusPatch`, exported from rail-adapters). The refresh service used to follow up with a second `application.update` of its own — which is where the stringified code came from — while its docblock claimed *"identical writes, identical event log rows"*. **A docblock asserting two paths agree is not a mechanism that makes them agree.**
- **The sponsor bank's own `upiPayReason` WINS over the code wording** when it sends one: it is the more specific of the two and the one an investor can act on. Blank/whitespace falls back to the label, never to an empty string.
- **`rejectionReason` was being passed and dropped on the floor.** `handlePayStatus`/`handleDpStatus` have always passed a reason and `PrismaApplicationRepo.updateStatus` wrote only status + amountBlocked + the event row, so a rejection's cause was readable only inside `ApplicationStatusEvent.detail` JSON. Now written, and **on failures only** — a stale reason beside a confirmed bid reads as a problem.
- **A malformed callback used to return 500.** A body missing `symbol`/`applicationNumber` reached `findBySymbolAndApplicationNo(undefined, undefined)` and Prisma threw on the `symbol: undefined` lookup — where the exchange's own doc requires `{ status: "failed", reason }`. **A 500 also tells an exchange to RETRY**, which is wrong for a body that can never succeed. `badKeys()` refuses before any database call.
  - **A MISSING flag is not an UNRECOGNISED one, and the distinction is the whole point.** `mapUpiStatus(NaN)` and `mapDpStatus(undefined)` both return a real status (`mandate_pending` / `submitted`), so a malformed callback would have quietly overwritten a live bid with a status derived from garbage. Missing/non-numeric is REFUSED; an unrecognised NUMBER still maps to pending, deliberately — that is forward compatibility with a code the exchange adds later.
- **Investor UI, web + mobile**: the sentence plus `As of <fmtDateTime> · from the exchange`, beside our own status line. **Tone comes from `status`, never from reading the sentence.** Web is `.upi-line` / `.upi-dot` in globals.css (new names — the existing `.upi-*` family is the profile form's split input) with the COLOUR on the 7px dot, not on text over a tint, because `--pos` on `--pos-soft` is 3.50:1 and fails AA. Mobile is `upiRow` in `app/(tabs)/applications.tsx`. **Keep the two in step by hand**, same as the split UPI input.
- **No consumer-facing Refresh button, deliberately.** `fetchTransactions` pulls a whole delta window per call, so one investor tap = a full exchange fetch — the load pattern the refresh service's docblock already warns about. The right fix is the per-bid pull (`ipoupistatus`), unimplemented. The push path makes polling unnecessary.
- **A dead check found in passing**: `missingDetails` tested `a.applyMethod === 'upi'`, a value the `ApplyMethod` enum (`native | pdf`) has NEVER held — so *"UPI ID missing"*, the one piece of advice an investor can act on, could never appear. `native` IS the UPI-ASBA path.
- **39 → 50 tests in rail-adapters**, incl. `rail-callback.service.spec.ts` with a fake repo asserting what each callback writes. They earned it immediately: a later edit of mine deleted the `app` lookup from both handlers and the spec caught it on the next run.
- **Proving a column write without fabricating data**: read a real row, write inside an interactive `$transaction`, read back, then THROW to roll back, then re-read outside to confirm it is clean. Worth it here because `upiStatusAt` is `timestamptz(3)` and this project has already been burned by a naive/aware mismatch on exactly this class of column — measured **0 ms drift**.
- **UNPROVEN END TO END, and it cannot be proved yet**: all 1,651 applications are print-path (`pdf`) with **0 exchange application numbers and 0 status events**, so no UPI event has ever flowed. The writes and wording are under test; the investor line renders only once a sentence exists. Blocked on NSE UAT creds like the rest of the bidding surface.

### UPI ID change, and the bid submission builder (2026-10-08)
- **`toSubmission()` IN `bid-operations.service.ts` RETURNED `any`, AND UNDER THAT `any` NO BID COULD EVER HAVE BEEN PLACED.** Against the `BidSubmission` contract the adapters actually post: **no `upi` field at all** — so `toAddPayload` throws *"Either upi or bankAccount is required"* **before any network call** · `clientId` where the contract says **`clientBenId`** · flat `quantity`/`price` where it says **`bids: Bid[]`**, so `req.bids.map` throws · **`pan: profile.pan`, a column `InvestorProfile` DOES NOT HAVE** (only `panTokenRef`/`panHash`), so always `undefined` · no `bidReferenceNumber`, which the spec requires on anything but a `new`. **Five fatal faults in one object literal, none visible to the compiler, none reachable by a test.** The lesson is the `any`: a return type of `any` on a function whose whole job is to satisfy a contract removes the only check that mattered.
- **`buildBidSubmission()` in `packages/rail-adapters/src/submission/build-bid-submission.ts` is now the ONE builder** — pure, over plain values, **24 tests from the spec's own sample request** (`/v1/transactions/add`, the UPI-flow example). It was untestable *because* it needed a Prisma row; moving it out is the same step that made `offeredFromIpo`, `parseAnchorRows` and `offerTotals` testable. **It REFUSES rather than sending a payload the exchange will reject**, with wording that names the applicant and the fix — the adapter's generic error arrives after a session has been opened. A malformed PAN is masked in the message (`NO****23`); never log a whole PAN.
- **PII is resolved JUST-IN-TIME and never stored on the operation row**, so a retry sends whatever the profile says now. **A failed vault resolve becomes an EMPTY STRING on purpose** — the builder's refusal names the applicant, which beats a vault stack trace on a closing-day queue.
- **`mayChangeUpi(status, atExchange)` in `bidRules.ts` has THREE outcomes, and the middle one is why it is a function**: not at the exchange → a plain edit, nothing to tell anyone · at the exchange, mandate not accepted → **queue a MODIFY carrying the new UPI** · **funds already blocked → REFUSED**, because the block sits in the account behind the OLD UPI ID and the exchange has no concept of moving it; the refusal names the alternative (withdraw and re-apply). **Category plays no part** — fixing a mistyped UPI ID is not a revision of the bid, so the ICDR up-only rule governing `mayReviseTo` does not apply and an HNI may do it. 6 tests.
- **`upi` IS AN APPLICANT-LEVEL FIELD** on `/transactions/add`, beside `pan` and `dpId` rather than inside the bid — which is the whole reason "change the UPI on a placed bid" is expressible at all: a modify restates it. (The spec also requires `timestamp` echoed from the last response on a modify; **not yet carried — check this before UAT.**)
