  - **`enrich()` FELL BACK TO `forms-for-1x × the SHARE-wise multiple`, which is not a count of applications at all.** It is how many 1×-sized applications' worth of SHARES were bid, so it over-counts wherever anyone bids more than the minimum — i.e. always. **And it read `sub.snii` / `sub.bnii`, keys this codebase has never stored**, so both HNI bands silently fell through to the NII ROLL-UP and neither band's own multiple was ever used. Two faults in four lines, invisible because the result was merely plausible.
  - **MEASURED BEFORE DELETING IT: of 1,304 visible records, ZERO lose a figure.** Every record the derivation could fire on also carries exchange bid counts, so it only ever spoke where it was wrong. 8 records carry the operator's own `applicationsReceived`, 35 carry exchange counts, 1,261 have neither and showed nothing before or after. **A fallback that can only fire where a better answer exists is not a fallback.**
  - Precedence is now **operator's stated figure → the exchange's own `total.bidCount` → nothing**, and the second of those is the EXACT expression the hub tile prints, so the two pages cannot drift. `f.appWise` went with it — it had no reader left once the derivation was gone.
  - **The tile and the panel total legitimately differ by QIB's bid count** (29,47,939 against 29,47,832): the tile is every category, the panel excludes QIB because QIB has no application multiple. Both pages show the same pair, which is what makes it readable rather than a disagreement.
- **TWO TONE LADDERS, AND THEY DISAGREED ABOUT THE WORD *AND* THE COLOUR.** At JPL's 3.00× `/subscription` said **Oversubscribed** in green while the detail page said **Heavily subscribed**; between **2× and 3× it was worse** — hot green on one page, warm amber on the other, for one issue. `tone()` / `toneLabel()` are exported from `SubscriptionHub` now and `LiveSubscription` imports them, the same rule as `demandWord()` / `demandTier()`. **The hub's copy is canonical because three surfaces already read it** — the hub cards, `/subscription/v2` and the shared image — and only the detail page differed.
  - **The BAR itself was never wrong**: both compute `(t/3)*100` and both measured 100% fill. **It was the word under it**, which is why "the bar is not as per the subscription page" needed a screenshot to diagnose rather than a measurement of the bar.
  - `fmtIn` and `fmtCr` are still re-stated locally in `LiveSubscription` — harmless today because they agree, and exactly the shape that produced this bug.
- **"Total applications" BECAME A REAL TOTAL ROW** (operator report 2) — `tfoot > tr.tot`, the same class the Share-wise table's total already carries, so two tables on one screen end the same way instead of one shouting and one whispering. It reaches **all three surfaces at once** because `AppWisePanel` is `/subscription`, `/subscription/v2` and the detail page. **The class carries the padding explicitly**: `.subv2-tbl tbody td` is scoped to tbody, which is exactly how the partner-api total ended up with its figures out of line with its body columns. Measured after: `tfoot 10px 5px 10px 2px` against `tbody 8px 5px 8px 2px`, weight 800 against 400, table 310.2 inside a 310 wrap.
  - The two ratio columns are deliberately **blank**: Req 1× is a per-category capacity and the multiples are ratios. Neither sums.
- **THE SHARED IMAGE WAS SIX COLUMNS AT 16px AND UNREADABLE ON A PHONE** (operator report 4, with a screenshot). The canvas is 1080px and a forwarded image is fitted to a chat window — roughly 400px — so everything is seen at about **37% of the size written**: 16px arrived as 6px. **Two of the six columns were the same quantity twice** (Shares/Book is the book size in shares and in rupees, Subs/Sub ₹ likewise), ~~so dropping one unit per pair lost nothing and paid for the type.~~ **REVERSED 2026-10-22 — the operator asked for the share counts back the next morning; see *The share snapshot became the page*. They went back as a second LINE, not a column, which is why the type size survived.** Figures 16 → **26px**, category 15 → **22px**, headers 11 → **15px**, totals 17 → **28px**, section titles 14 → **19px**.
  - **IT WAS RENDERING THE API'S RAW ORDER.** `ShareWisePanel` and `DemandPanel` both sort by `SHARE_ORDER`; the canvas did not, so a shared image read Employee, HNI (2-10L), Retail… against QIB, NII, HNI (10L+) on the page it came from. That is the whole of report 4's *"order must as per subscription page"*.
  - **THE DISCLAIMER TRAVELS WITH THE IMAGE.** A forwarded screenshot leaves the page and its caveat behind, so a figure that carries one on site has to carry it there. **BUT IT SHIPPED UNREADABLE RATHER THAN ABSENT** (found 2026-10-22): `.sc-disclaimer` was declared once, UNSCOPED, at 15px among the showcase rules, so it rendered at a third of the figures’ size — about 5px forwarded. **Adding a block and scoping its rule are two jobs**; the first one alone looks finished.
  - **MEASURING THE WRONG CELL REPORTED THE CHANGE AS NOT LANDING.** `sec.querySelector('tbody td')` is `:first-child` — the CATEGORY cell, which has a rule of its own — so the first pass read 15px and concluded the 26px had not applied. **Measure per column.** Same family as the 12 KB decoy stylesheet.
  - **One of my own rules landed UNSCOPED** — a bare `.sc-tbl td:first-child` where every other rule in that block is prefixed `.subv2-canvas`. Redundant and exactly the collision `globals.css` has been bitten by four times. Removed.
- **THE SHARE CANVAS IS MOUNTED, RASTERISED AND REMOVED INSIDE ONE HANDLER**, so a look two seconds after the click finds nothing. **Arm a `MutationObserver` BEFORE clicking and clone the node the instant it appears** — that is the same DOM html2canvas is handed, kept long enough to measure and photograph. **APPEND THE CLONE: a DETACHED node returns an EMPTY STRING for every computed style**, so a measuring pass that runs after the handler clears the card reads nothing at all and looks like a failed render (2026-10-22). The share button exists only where `onShare` is passed, i.e. `/subscription`, never `/subscription/v2`.
- **THERE IS NO PUPPETEER ON THIS BOX AND IT DOES NOT NEED ONE.** Chrome is installed and speaks CDP over a WebSocket, and `ws` is already a dependency — `scratchpad/cdp.js` is a ~90-line driver with `goto`/`evaluate`/`shot`/`viewport`. Launch it with **`--host-resolver-rules="MAP newipoapi.finwave.co 127.0.0.1"` plus `--ignore-certificate-errors`**: this box cannot reach its own public address (NAT hairpinning) but IIS serves the right site by SNI over loopback, so a client-side fetch really lands. **A stub installed after navigating is destroyed by the next navigation** — use `Page.addScriptToEvaluateOnNewDocument` when something must run before page script.
- **`python` IS NOT ON THIS BOX** (`node` is). And the harness ate a heredoc again — a quoted `<<'EOF'` died on an unterminated quote and `$CLAUDE_SCRATCH` is not set; **the Write tool plus `node script.js` is the reliable shape for a patch script**, as this file has now recorded three times.

## `/attributions` — the open-data licences (2026-10-21)
- **CC BY 4.0 WANTS TWO THINGS AND THE OBVIOUS READING GIVES ONLY ONE.** Credit and a link are the half everybody remembers; clause **3(a)(1)(B) also requires that MODIFICATIONS BE INDICATED**, and ours are substantial — GeoNames publishes **one row per locality** and our table holds **one row per pincode**, taking the most frequent district and state. So *"pincode data from GeoNames"* would have satisfied half the licence. The page says what was changed, in the words a reader can check: the collapse, the majority rule, the `ambiguous` flag, the Puducherry rename and Ladakh carried as a selectable state.
- **THE MIT NOTICE IS REPRODUCED VERBATIM, NOT LINKED.** razorpay/ifsc is MIT and MIT says *"The above copyright notice and this permission notice shall be included in all copies or substantial portions"* — **included**, which a hyperlink is not. It is set in `.attr-notice` so it reads as a quotation rather than as our prose.
- **BOTH LICENCE TEXTS WERE READ FROM THE SOURCES, NOT RECALLED** (2026-10-21): `raw.githubusercontent.com/razorpay/ifsc/master/LICENSE` gives **`Copyright (c) 2020 Razorpay`**, and `download.geonames.org/export/zip/readme.txt` states CC BY 4.0 and says outright *"a link on your website to www.geonames.org is ok"* — which is also what CC BY 4.0 §3(a)(2) contemplates, so a footer link to this page discharges it. **The source files were no longer on this box, so a copyright line could not have been verified from disk** — fetch it rather than writing a year and a holder from memory.
- **IT IS NOT A `TrustPage`, AND THAT IS THE WHOLE POINT.** Every other page in that family is per-tenant rich text a white-label replaces, and an unpublished one renders *"not yet published"*. This must do neither: **a partner serves the SAME directory off the SAME database, so the obligation is theirs as much as ours.** Plain static markup, identical on every host, deliberately OUTSIDE `[data-own-only]`, and the footer link sits in the **ungated legal row** rather than behind `own`. Hiding it on a partner's domain would leave them publishing CC-BY data attributing nobody. Verified against `?brand=finwave`: page renders in full, notice present, footer link present and visible.
- **THE NOTICE RENDERED AT THE WRONG SIZE AND THE RULE LOOKED FINE.** `.trust-page .attr-notice` is **(0,2,0)** while `.trust-page .prose p` is **(0,2,1)** AND sits later in the file, so the block took the body's 14.5px — losing on specificity and on order at once. `.trust-page .prose p.attr-notice` wins. **Measured in the built page, not read off the rule**, which is the standing rule here and the same trap as `.fin-tab th:first-child`.
- Sitemap entry added at 0.2/yearly beside the other trust pages. Phone width measured: **no horizontal overflow at 375**, the licence block fits at 332 in 360.

## `providers.manage`, and a hole in every slug-less route (2026-10-21)
- **READ THE NEXT THREE BULLETS AS A SEQUENCE, NOT AS THE CURRENT STATE.** They record a decision changing twice in one afternoon, which is worth keeping — but **as of the end of 2026-10-21 all six partner Admins hold `providers.manage` AND the white-label gate is gone**, so where a bullet below says an ordinary partner would get "a screen on which every save fails", that stopped being true the same day. The last two bullets in this section are what holds.
- **THE OPEN ITEM WAS RAISED ON A WRONG PREMISE, and checking it is what found the real fault.** *"One partner has `providers.manage` and five do not"* reads as drift. It is not: **`providers.manage` is in `WHITELABEL_ADMIN_PERMS` and NOT in `PARTNER_ADMIN_PERMS`**, because a white-label publishes under its own brand and configures its own SMTP / SMS / WhatsApp. Measured: **2 of the 6 partners are white-label** (acml, finwave) and 4 are not. **Granting it to an ordinary partner would not widen their reach — `ProviderConfigService.assertConfigurable` refuses a non-white-label tenant outright, so they would get a screen on which every save fails.** Worse than not having it.
  - **The REAL gap was the opposite one: FINWAVE is white-label and was missing SEVEN permissions**, `providers.manage` among them — and FINWAVE is the partner running the live `newipo.finwave.co` domain, so they publish a site to their own clients and could not configure the mail that site sends. **SIHL was missing four** against its own partner template.
  - **BOTH PREDATE THE CURRENT LISTS, and that is a standing trap**: `registerTenant` stamps a role's permissions ONCE at creation and nothing re-applies them, so every later addition leaves every existing role behind. Exactly the `tenant.site.manage` backfill gap of 2026-10-16, recurring. **`scripts/backfill-partner-admin-perms.js`** (dry-run by default, ADDITIVE only, chooses the template from the tenant's own `flags.whitelabel`) is the reusable answer; re-running it now reports *"every partner Admin matches its template"*.
- **THE HOLE: `PermissionsGuard` CANNOT SCOPE A ROUTE THAT HAS NO `:slug`, AND SAYS SO IN A COMMENT.** It resolves the target tenant from the `:slug` param and then `if (!target) return true; // has the permission somewhere`. For a route confined to the caller's own tenant that is right. For one that writes the PLATFORM's own configuration the permission becomes the entire check, whatever the holder's scope.
  - **Measured on production as a partner Admin (scope `subtree`), BEFORE → AFTER**: `GET /admin/providers` **200 → 403** · `GET /admin/integrations/overview` **200 → 403** · `GET /admin/chatbot` **200 → 403** · their OWN ``/admin/tenants/<own>/providers`` **200 → 200**, and another partner's **403 throughout**. So **the per-tenant half was always sound and the platform half was never guarded at all.**
  - **WHAT IT REACHED.** `PUT /admin/providers/subscription` is the subscription poller's cadence and window **for every issue on the platform** — and an unpolled bidding hour is the irreversible loss this file documents everywhere. Also our SMTP, our Anthropic key, the SMS provider, every partner's integration state, and `/admin/chatbot`, which is **our** WhatsApp bot's menu, FAQs and greeting in one copy for everybody.
  - **THE HAZARD WAS ALREADY WRITTEN DOWN, ONE SCREEN AWAY.** The docblock on `AdminService.assertSuperadmin` reads *"the guard alone would pass any tenants.manage holder on slug-less routes"* — about the message-type catalog, beside these routes, where the same reasoning was never applied. **A hazard noted at one call site is not a hazard fixed at the next.**
  - **`SuperadminGuard` (`src/common/superadmin.guard.ts`) is that check made reusable** — scope `all`, read unscoped for the same reason the login lookups are. Stack it after `PermissionsGuard` on any slug-less platform route. One refusal message whichever way it failed: which of *"you are not a platform admin"* and *"this route is platform-only"* applies is not something a tenant-scoped caller needs told apart.
- **ALL SIX PARTNER ADMINS NOW HOLD `providers.manage` — OPERATOR DECISION, taken after the trade-off was put to them** (2026-10-21). `scripts/grant-providers-manage.js`, kept SEPARATE from `backfill-partner-admin-perms.js` precisely because it departs from the template: mixing them would make a deliberate exception look like routine drift the next time somebody diffs the roles.
  - **WHAT IT BUYS THE FOUR NON-WHITE-LABEL PARTNERS IS THE SCREEN, NOT THE SAVE**, and that was measured rather than predicted. SIHL, holding it: `GET /admin/tenants/sihl/providers` **200** (the page loads and shows what it inherits) · any write **403, "Only white-label tenants can configure their own provider keys"**, from `assertConfigurable`, which admits the platform tenant and `flags.whitelabel` tenants only. ACML, white-label, writes fine (**200**) on the identical probe. **So four partners now have a menu entry they can open and not use** — the "button that can only fail" shape, accepted deliberately here rather than arrived at by accident.
  - ~~**THE GATE WAS DELIBERATELY LEFT ALONE.**~~ **SUPERSEDED LATER THE SAME DAY — the operator asked for it and it is gone; see the next bullet.** The reasoning is kept because it is still the reasoning: relaxing `assertConfigurable` means deciding whether an ordinary partner — whose clients receive mail under OUR brand — should send it through their own server, which is a different question from a white-label doing it under their own. **A permission grant must not answer that by side effect**, which is why it was put to the operator rather than bundled in.
  - **THE PROBE THAT TESTS A WRITE GATE WITHOUT WRITING: delete a provider config the tenant does not have.** `deleteForTenant` calls `assertConfigurable` FIRST, so a refusal proves the gate and a pass is a no-op over zero rows. Measured first that all six tenants inherit the platform config and own none, so nothing could be lost either way.
  - Containment is unchanged by the grant, re-measured after it: platform routes **403**, another partner **403**. The permission reaches the holder’s own tenant and stops there.
- **THE WHITE-LABEL GATE ON PROVIDER KEYS IS GONE — OPERATOR DECISION, and the "one line" was THREE PLACES** (2026-10-21). `assertConfigurable` admitted the platform tenant and `flags.whitelabel` tenants only; the operator wants every partner running their own SMS and mail. Removing the flag check is the obvious edit and would have been the least useful third of the change:
  - **`integrationsOverview` SELECTED `flags.whitelabel = true` TENANTS.** The moment an ordinary partner could configure anything, that filter became a BLIND SPOT — a partner routing their clients’ OTPs and mail through their own gateway would not have appeared on the one screen that exists to show who is doing that. It now lists any partner or branch that is white-label **OR has actually configured something**, and each row carries `whitelabel` so the two kinds are distinguishable. **Not simply every tenant**: padding the audit view with partners who have configured nothing is how a page stops being read.
  - **THE ADMIN SCOPE PICKER (`app/admin/integrations/page.tsx`) LISTED WHITE-LABEL TENANTS ONLY**, so the API would have accepted the write and **the dropdown offered the operator nowhere to make it** for four of the six partners. An ordinary partner now reads **"Name (our brand)"** — marked rather than filtered, because *whose brand the mail goes out under* is the thing to know before touching somebody’s SMTP.
  - **THE TENANT-EXISTS CHECK STAYS and is now the whole method.** It is what stops a config being written against an id that names nothing, which `effective()` would never resolve and nobody would ever see. (In practice `PermissionsGuard` refuses an unknown slug first, with 403 rather than 404 — which also declines to tell a partner whether a tenant exists.)
  - **Scoping never lived in that flag and still does not.** Re-measured after the change, as SIHL: own provider write **200** (was 403) · platform **403** · another partner **403** · unknown tenant **403**.
  - **PROVED THE OVERVIEW NO LONGER GOES BLIND, with an INERT row**: a `providerConfig` written `enabled: false`, which `effective()` skips (it takes the nearest ENABLED config) so no live traffic could route through it. SIHL appeared in the overview marked `whitelabel: false`, and the row was deleted. **An audit view is worth testing by making it see something, not by reading its WHERE clause.**
  - **THE LESSON IS THE COUNT.** One deliberate gate had three readers — the writer, the auditor and the way in — and only the first announces itself when you change it. **Grep for every reader of a rule before calling its removal one line.** This file has recorded the same shape for `sharesUpper` (four readers, two contradicting the convention) and for `shareResv[].on`.
- **TWO FULL API INSTANCES EXHAUST POSTGRES, and the `dist-check` note does not warn about it.** That pattern is documented for the second POLLER; the second **connection pool** is the other half. Running `dist-check` on :3999 beside the live pool produced *"Too many database connections opened: FATAL: remaining connection slots are reserved for roles with the SUPERUSER attribute"*, and the heaviest route — `/admin/integrations/overview`, which walks every tenant that is white-label or has configured something — was the first to 500. **It read exactly like a bug in the change under test.** Proved otherwise by elimination: kill the second instance, same token, same route, **200**. Mint any tokens you need from ONE client before starting the second instance, and never open a Prisma client per probe.
- **Permissions resolve fresh from Membership → Role on every request**, so a role edit takes effect immediately and the backfill needed no pool restart. That is the same property `JwtAuthGuard`'s subject lookup relies on.

## The share snapshot became the page (2026-10-22)
- **NINE OPERATOR POINTS, AND THREE OF THEM HAD ONE CAUSE — the card named two fonts that do not exist on the page it renders on.** See the next section; it turned out to be the whole site's problem, not the card's. Everything below is the rest of the list.
- **WIDTH IS THE ONLY DIMENSION THAT COSTS LEGIBILITY, AND THAT RESOLVES THE APPARENT CONTRADICTION.** The operator asked for MORE content and BIGGER type in the same message. The card is a fixed **1080px** and a chat fits an image **to width**, so a forward is read at **37%** — measured, not estimated: a 400px chat view of a 2160px render. So **extra ROWS are free and extra COLUMNS are not.** The share counts went back in as a SECOND LINE inside the existing cell, exactly as `ShareWisePanel` renders them, adding no column.
  - That reverses the 2026-10-21 trade directly above, and the reversal came the next morning. **A column cut that "loses nothing" because the other unit is still shown is a claim about what the reader wants, not about the data** — the operator wanted the share counts, and the rupees were the half that could have gone.
  - Final shape **1080 × 2188 (1:2.0)**, operator's choice of four offered. **4:5 is not reachable with this content** and that was measured rather than argued: the two tables alone are 1,264px. Dropping the fine print gives 1:1.8, dropping Application-wise 1:1.5.
- **THE DISCLAIMER WAS UNREADABLE RATHER THAN ABSENT, which is the harder failure to notice.** `.sc-disclaimer` was declared ONCE, **UNSCOPED**, at 15px among the `.showcase` rules — there was no `.subv2-canvas` rule for it at all — so it rendered at a third of the figures' size, about **5px** once forwarded. The sixth instance of the `globals.css` collision family, and mine from the day before. It now leads with the operative sentence at a readable size and carries the operator's **locked** wording (2026-09-18, `SubscriptionDisclaimer`) beneath as the record.
- **THE FOOTER TIMESTAMP WAS `new Date()` — WHEN SHARE WAS PRESSED, NOT THE AGE OF THE FIGURES.** A snapshot taken at 18:00 of a book last polled at 13:52 claimed 18:00. It reads `subscriptionAsOf` now, beside the figures, with the generated time keeping its own labelled line. **Two timestamps that mean different things must both say which they are**; one of them says nothing about the numbers.
- **THE COMPANY LOGO FIELD IS `logoUrl`, AND MEASURING `logo` REPORTS ZERO.** I told the operator no IPO carries a logo — **0 of 1,306** — from the raw API payload. The column is `Ipo.logoUrl` and `enrich()` in `apps/web/lib/api.ts` maps it to `logo` for the cards; the real answer is **102 of 1,306, including both issues open that day**. **Measure the field the SURFACE reads, or the field the API sends, but know which one you have** — an `as any` cast on either side hides the difference from the compiler.
- **`useCORS: true`, NEVER `allowTaint`.** The company logo is served from the API host, which answers `Access-Control-Allow-Origin` for our origins **and for localhost** (`LOCAL` in `main.ts`), which is also what makes it verifiable off a local build. `allowTaint` would draw the image and then TAINT the canvas, so `toBlob` throws and **sharing breaks outright** — strictly worse than a missing logo. Both logos are preloaded and `document.fonts.ready` awaited before rasterising, because html2canvas reads the live DOM and gets no second chance.
- **`.sc-x` WAS RENAMED TO `.sc-mult` BEFORE IT SHIPPED.** It is already defined three times unscoped in `globals.css`, and the canvas block declares **no `font-family` at all** on purpose — so that global rule would have reached into the Times column and put the display face back. Every other new class name was checked the same way; that was the only hit. **A scoped block that deliberately omits a property is MORE exposed to a name collision, not less.**
- **Carried over from the page because the image is meant to BE the page**: `QIB (Ex. Anchor)` and the anchor footnote (without them a shared QIB book is short by the anchor with nothing explaining it), the `nii` roll-up printed as **HNI** rather than the raw API key, the two HNI bands tinted and indented as the SUB-ROWS they are instead of three peers whose multiples do not reconcile, and the page's own header wording. `subscriptionTable` already returns `anchorDeducted`/`anchorBasis`, so the footnote cost no arithmetic.
- **HOW TO RENDER AND MEASURE THE REAL CARD, which is the reusable part.** Serve the BUILT export over a local static server and drive headless Chrome over CDP with **`--host-resolver-rules="MAP newipoapi.finwave.co 127.0.0.1"`** and `--ignore-certificate-errors`: this box cannot reach its own public address (NAT), but IIS routes by SNI over loopback, so the catalogue fetch AND the cross-origin logo resolve for real. Then:
  - **Wrap `HTMLCanvasElement.prototype.toBlob` before clicking** and keep `this.toDataURL()`. That captures the exact canvas the app produced, and because `toDataURL` throws the same `SecurityError` a tainted canvas would, **it doubles as the proof that CORS held.**
  - **A DETACHED NODE RETURNS EMPTY COMPUTED STYLES.** The first measuring pass read `""` for every font and size, because the handler removes the card 800ms later and the measurement ran after. **Clone the node the instant it appears AND APPEND THE CLONE** — same classes, same CSS, inline styles carried — then measure the clone.
  - **`next dev` is not an option for this** (it leaves `<SYMBOL>.html` DIRECTORIES that kill the next export), and a hand-copied HTML harness verifies the CSS while exercising none of the data path — the fault already written up on the featured panel.

## The site loaded two webfonts and painted neither (2026-10-22)
- **`next/font` SETS `--font-sans` AND `--font-display` ON `<html>` THROUGH A GENERATED CLASS, SPECIFICITY (0,1,0). `globals.css` THEN RE-DECLARED BOTH AT `:root`, ALSO (0,1,0)** — and this file is linked AFTER the font sheet, so the tie went to source order and **the fallback won on every page of the site, since the stylesheet was written.**
  - Measured before the change on the built export, across home · subscription · a detail page · terms · admin: `--font-sans` resolves to the system stack, **body and every heading compute to it**, all four faces report **`unloaded`**, and **ZERO `.woff2` files are fetched.** So the site rendered in Segoe UI on Windows, Roboto on Android and SF on iOS — **our typography changed with the reader's device**, which is the opposite of what shipping a webfont is for.
- **THE FIX IS THE SELECTOR, NOT THE VALUE.** The two declarations moved to **`html` (0,0,1)**, which loses to a class whatever the load order — so `next/font` wins when present and this stays a real fallback when it is not. **Keeping a fallback matters**: with no declaration anywhere, `var(--font-display)` is invalid at computed-value time and `font-family` lands on the INHERITED value rather than on anything chosen.
- After, on the published build: **Inter** on body, **Plus Jakarta Sans** on headings, on all five pages, and **four `.woff2` files actually fetched**. `:root{--font-sans` appears 0 times in the published CSS, `html{--font-sans` once.
- **A FONT SWAP IS A REFLOW RISK, NOT A COLOUR ONE — so sweep for overflow, both builds.** 18 consumer pages at **1440 and 375**: exactly one page overflows horizontally and it overflows on **BOTH**, so the change introduces nothing. The sweep is `document.documentElement.scrollWidth - clientWidth` per page, with the worst offending element's ancestor chain when it is non-zero.
- **IT WAS FOUND FROM THE SHARE CARD.** Fixing the card's fonts is what exposed that the page underneath had the same disease — the card resolves its family from the live DOM, so the moment it stopped asking for a literal name it started reporting what the page actually paints. **The card now tracks the page automatically**: it re-rendered in Inter after this deploy with no change of its own.

## Admin → Subscription Report (2026-10-22)
- **WHY IT EXISTS: `/subscription` only ever showed issues that are open RIGHT NOW.** The day-wise table and its hourly drill-down were unreachable for anything closed, so the back catalogue could not be read at all. This screen is a picker plus the same two components.
- **IT RENDERS THE PUBLIC COMPONENTS, NOT ADMIN COPIES.** `IpoRow` brings Share-wise, Application-wise and the lot ladder; `DayWiseSubscription` brings the timeline. Re-implementing either would put a second opinion of the same figures on a second screen — the exact fault that had the detail page and the hub disagreeing about Applications and about the tone word. **A picker and a shell is the whole page.**
- **TWO DATA TIERS, AND THE SECOND ONE CANNOT BE FIXED** (measured 2026-10-22, from the database and the API payload independently): **32 IPOs carry a day-wise log** and 29 of those an hourly one · **298 carry a FINAL SNAPSHOT ONLY** · 977 carry nothing. **`IpoSubscription` is `deleteMany` + `createMany` on every poll**, so an older issue’s timeline was never stored and there is nothing to reconstruct it from. **The archive begins 18 Sep 2026.** The picker says which tier an issue is in and the empty state says why, so a missing timeline reads as a known limit rather than a bug.
  - A normal three-day issue keeps **3 day entries and 24 hourly buckets**, so the 14-day and 72-entry caps never bite — recent issues are complete, not truncated.
  - The picker lists **only the 330 issues that have subscription data** (operator’s call): the other 977 would be options that always render nothing.
- **THE CSV INTERLEAVES DAY AND HOUR UNDER A `Scope` COLUMN**, one file rather than two, so a sheet can filter it either way; BOM first, or Excel reads the system codepage. **Column mapping was cross-checked against a RENDERED row rather than against itself**: HDFIRE’s last day reads qib 102.66 · hni 236.17 · hni2 137.28 · retail 30.49 · total 92.3 in the CSV and the identical five figures in the page’s own table.
- **AN AUTH-GATED PAGE CAN STILL BE VERIFIED, BY RENDERING ITS COMPONENTS SOMEWHERE PUBLIC.** `/auth/me` supplies the permissions, so proving the admin shell would have meant creating a throwaway operator AND a throwaway role. Instead the risky half — do `IpoRow` and `DayWiseSubscription` survive a CLOSED issue — was proved on **`/subscription/v2?symbol=HDFIRE`**, which is public and uses the same two components on the same data: card present, Share-wise present, **3 day rows with 3 hourly chevrons**, 0 overflow. **Find the unauthenticated surface that exercises the same code before building a login to test one.**
- `istTodayYmd` was duplicated in `SubscriptionHub` and `NseIpoLanding`; it is **exported from the hub now and imported by both others**, so adding a third consumer REMOVED a copy instead of adding one.
- Sidebar: **IPO Management → Subscription Report**, gated `ipos.view` like the rest of that group, so Staff see it too.

## The language switcher is hidden, and only the switcher (2026-10-22)
- **THE CONTROL IS GONE; NOTHING ELSE IS.** `/hi` pages are still built (**500 IPO pages plus the home**), still carry their **1,503 hreflang alternates** in the sitemap, and `?lang=hi` still resolves — so anyone arriving from search is unaffected and nothing is de-indexed. Restoring it is **one commented line** in `SiteHeader`; the component is untouched.
- **TWO MECHANISMS COEXIST and it is worth knowing before anyone "finishes" this**: real per-locale ROUTES (`/hi/`, for the home page and `/ipos/*`) and a `?lang=` QUERY PARAM for everything else. `LangSwitcher` chose between them by path.
- **THE HINDI PAGES ARE BARELY TRANSLATED, measured rather than assumed**: the Hindi home carries **236 Devanagari characters against 22** on the English one — a handful of swapped strings in a 5.8 MB page. So a Hindi searcher already lands on an essentially English page; hiding the switcher does not change what they see, only that they cannot switch back. **The hreflang side is correctly built** (sitemap `xhtml:link` alternates, en/hi/x-default per URL) and is the part worth keeping if the translation pass ever lands.

## The subscription layout, reviewed (2026-10-22 → 23)
- **A REVIEWER SENT FIVE POINTS AND A MOCKUP. TWO WERE ALREADY SHIPPED, ONE WAS WRONG, THREE WERE WORTH DOING** — and the one he pressed hardest was the wrong one. Worth reading before acting on the next design note: **check each claim against the page before agreeing with the confident ones.**
- **THE "DATA-QUALITY ISSUE" WAS NOT ONE.** He reported the header Applications tile and the Application-wise total differing by 212 and asked for them to be reconciled. The gap is **exactly QIB’s bid count** — the tile is `total.bidCount`, every category, while the panel excludes QIB because institutions bid without a minimum application size and so have no applications-for-1× to divide by. **Two correct totals a reader cannot reconcile still look like an error**, which is a presentation question. A footnote explaining it shipped for one day and the operator removed it (2026-10-23); the reasoning now lives as a comment on `AppWisePanel` so it is not re-opened as a bug.
- **ALREADY DONE, so nothing to do**: his colour-discipline point (category colours are a legend, green/amber only on the times column) and his "do not imply application count determines allotment" point — which was the operator’s own ask of 2026-10-21, shipped as the `NA` / `—` pro-rata rows **visible in his own screenshot**.
- **TABULAR NUMERALS WERE GENUINELY MISSING.** This file states the convention and 86 rules follow it; `.subv2-tbl` computed `normal`. One declaration, four surfaces.
- **THE DEMAND COLUMN TOOK THREE GOES AND THE THIRD FOUND THE REAL CULPRIT.** A 290px floor shipped on 2026-10-22, and the next morning the operator reported a horizontal scrollbar on Share-wise — measured: panel 408 against a 399px table in a 390px wrap, **9px short**. The floor had taken them. Reverting it was not the end of it either: the header BADGES added that day widened the Demand track by 34px on their own, because **two of these tracks are `auto` and an `auto` track sizes to MAX-CONTENT — including the header**. Third time this file has booked that mechanism, after the footnote that crushed a card to 69px and the detail page that pushed the whole site sideways.
  - **THE FIX IS THE DOCUMENTED IDIOM, APPLIED TO THE HEADER**: `width: 0` so it contributes nothing while the track is measured, `min-width: 100%` so it fills the column once that is settled. **The TABLE decides how wide a column is; the header just lives in it.** With the header out of the way Share-wise settled at 489 against a 417px need — 72px of genuine slack — and a floor can be paid for out of that.
  - **255px, NOT 270, AND THE REASON IS NOT THE VIEWPORT.** Measured at this width: 255 leaves 26px of headroom, 270 leaves 11, **285 already costs Share-wise 4px and 300 costs 19**. The grid is a capped ~1114px at EVERY viewport above the breakpoint — 1401, 1440 and 1920 all measure identically — so the margin is never eaten by a narrower window. It is eaten by **the figures growing**: a digit added to the Share-wise numbers costs about 14px across its two numeric columns, which 26px absorbs and 11px does not.
- **THE LOT LADDER WENT TO A FULL-WIDTH FOOTER AND CAME BACK THE NEXT DAY — BUT NOT ALL THE WAY BACK, AND THE HALF THAT STAYED IS THE POINT.** The operator did not like the footer and asked for it under Application-wise again (2026-10-23). It is there by **explicit grid placement**, NOT by restoring the `.subv2-det-stack` wrapper: `grid-column: 2; grid-row: 2` inside a `min-width: 1401px` block, scoped `:not(.subv2-detail)` because the detail page runs a TWO-column grid and forcing a panel into column 3 there conjures an implicit third. So the ladder is back where it looked right **and stays LAST in the DOM**, which is what a screen reader follows and what made the `order:` rules redundant.
  - **A REVERT IS A CHANCE TO KEEP THE HALF THAT WORKED.** The footer version was argued for on two grounds — how it looked and what the DOM order gave. Only the first was rejected, so only the first was undone. **Ask which half of a change the objection is actually about.**
- **PANEL HEADERS ARE A TINTED BAND WITH A SOLID BADGE, matching the mockup** (operator, 2026-10-23, over two rounds — tinted tiles first, then “as per mockup”). Each card gets one identity colour: a soft band bleeding the full card width, a 28px solid circle, a WHITE FILLED glyph. The four `-fill` variants were added to `Icon` beside the existing `star-fill` / `clock-fill` family; the outline versions read thin as a knockout on a solid badge.
  - **THE MOCKUP’S GOLD FAILS AND THE REST PASS.** White glyphs are graphics, so the bar is 3:1, and each badge was measured: indigo **11.15**, green **3.97**, blue **5.34** — but the gold is **2.03** and the glyph disappears into it. The ladder takes the deeper amber at **3.63**: same family, dark enough to read. **Match a mockup’s intent, not its hex, where the hex does not pass.**
  - **GREEN IS USED HERE, AND IT IS A LEGEND COLOUR RATHER THAN A SIGNAL.** It was teal for a day to stay clear of the gain/loss palette; the operator asked for the mockup. These colours name a CARD — they sit in the header band and never beside a figure — which is the same job the category swatches do in the tables. **Worth re-reading if green ever starts reading as “good” on the Application-wise card.**
  - **THE SUBTITLE STACKS UNDER THE TITLE** (`.pnl-ttl`). Inline, “in ₹ crore” wrapped to its own line anyway once the column narrowed — just raggedly, which is what the operator saw. **The band bleeds past the panel padding, so `min-width` is `calc(100% + 28px)`**: 100% resolves against the CONTENT box and the band needs the 14px of padding on each side too. `width: 0` still does the real work — without it the header sizes the `auto` track and starves its neighbour, which is exactly what the badges did on their first outing.
  - **THE BAND OVERHUNG THE CARD ON A PHONE, because it pulled a hardcoded margin against a padding that changes FOUR TIMES.** `.subv2-det-panel` pads `12px 14px` by default and `8px`, `10px 12px` and `8px 10px` at three breakpoints; the band pulled `-12px -14px` always, so at 375 the tint sat **5px outside the card on each side**. It now derives from `--pnl-px` / `--pnl-py`, which the breakpoints override INSTEAD of `padding`, so the bleed can never disagree again. Measured after: bleed is exactly the 1px border at 375, 414 and 768. **A value repeated in two rules is a value that will be changed in one.**
  - **APPLICATION-WISE IS THE ONE PANEL THAT STILL DRAGS SIDEWAYS ON A PHONE** — 52px inside its own card at 375 (Share-wise stacks into per-category blocks below 480, Demand and the ladder fit). Usable, since the wrapper scrolls, but it is the panel to stack next if the phone view is revisited. A CSS comment claimed all three fit; it was measured and corrected.
- ~~**OPEN: `DemandPanel` prints its ₹ crore figures without thousands separators**~~ **FIXED 2026-10-23.** It was `toFixed(2)`, which groups nothing — `167140.28` at five and six figures, and the one place the reviewer’s mockup was plainly better than the page. Now `toLocaleString('en-IN')` at two decimals: **1,67,140.28**, the lakh grouping every other figure on this site uses.
  - **KEPT LOCAL RATHER THAN FOLDED INTO `fmtCr`, deliberately.** This column prints BARE crore — no symbol, no rounding — because the panel heading already says *in ₹ crore*, while `fmtCr` adds the symbol AND rounds above 1000. Reusing it would have turned 1,67,140.28 into ₹1,67,140 Cr in a column headed *in ₹ crore*.
  - **Commas make strings longer, so the column was re-measured rather than assumed**: panel still 255px, squeeze 0, no page overflow. The 255px floor absorbed them.
- **DELIBERATELY NOT DONE: his "compact interpretation panel" to even out the column heights.** Equal heights is one line (`align-items: stretch`) but he explicitly did not want stretched empty cards, so his version means FINDING CONTENT — and a panel that interprets demand-by-application against demand-by-share is a short step from advice, which **Distribute + inform, don’t advise** rules out without an RIA registration. If it is ever built it states facts, not conclusions.
